Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a final summary report.

## Summary Report: "Apply for Official Status" Feature Analysis

Based on my thorough analysis of the codebase, here's the complete picture of the feature and the root cause of the error:

### 1. **Frontend Component (User-facing Submit UI)**

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/components/approvals/RequestOfficialDialog.vue` (lines 90-124)

When the user clicks the "Apply for Official" button and submits the form:
- The dialog captures: `extensionId`, `requestedTier` (productLine or company), `subCat`, optional `productLineId`, and optional `reason`
- Uses **`$fetch()` with a relative path**: `/api/internal/approvals/submit` (line 96)
- This is a standard Nuxt $fetch call that auto-resolves to the local server

**Note:** The UI works fine — the problem is not here.

### 2. **Server-side API Endpoint (Handler)**

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/server/api/internal/approvals/submit.post.ts` (lines 16-43)

This handler:
- Validates the request body against `SubmitApprovalSchema` (line 18-20)
- Calls `submitRequest()` from `server/utils/approvals.ts` (lines 23-30)
- On success, returns the approval request row (line 31)
- **On error, catches exceptions and logs:** `console.error("internal submitRequest failed", err)` (line 37)

### 3. **Client-side Integration**

The client (`RequestOfficialDialog.vue`) uses a **relative path** (`/api/internal/approvals/submit`) with `$fetch()`. This correctly resolves to the local Nitro server — no URL building issues here.

### 4. **Related Validators**

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/validators/approvals.ts` (lines 65-77)

The `SubmitApprovalSchema` validates:
- `extensionId` (non-empty string)
- `requestedTier` (enum: "productLine" | "company")
- `subCat` (must be in FUNC_TAXONOMY.l1)
- `productLineId` (optional, but required iff tier="productLine")
- `reason` (optional, max 500 chars)

### 5. **Database Schema**

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/shared/db/schema/approval.ts` (lines 22-72)

The `approvalRequests` table stores:
- Standard audit fields (id, extensionId, requestedTier, subCat, productLineId, requestedByUserId, reason)
- Status tracking (status: "pending" | "approved" | "rejected" | "withdrawn")
- Decision tracking (decidedByUserId, decidedAt, reviewerNote)
- Shape invariant: `productLineId IS NOT NULL iff requestedTier='productLine'` (enforced by CHECK constraint)

---

## Root Cause of the Error

The error message indicates an **Inngest client connection failure**:

```
ERROR  internal submitRequest failed fetch failed
[cause]: at internalConnectMultiple (node:net:1193:18)
```

### **Root Cause: Missing Inngest Dev Server**

When a request is submitted, `submitRequest()` in `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/server/utils/approvals.ts` (lines 96-106) calls:

```typescript
await inngest.send({
  name: "extension/approval.requested",
  data: { ... }
})
```

The Inngest client is configured in `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/server/utils/inngest.ts` (lines 3-7):

```typescript
export const inngest = new Inngest({
  id: "agentcenter",
  ...(process.env.INNGEST_EVENT_KEY ? { eventKey: process.env.INNGEST_EVENT_KEY } : {}),
  isDev: process.env.NODE_ENV !== "production",
})
```

**Key issue:** 
- `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY` is **empty** (see `.env` file, line 29: `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY=""`)
- `NODE_ENV` is likely `development` (not `production`)
- Therefore `isDev: true`, and the client tries to **connect to a local Inngest dev server**
- The dev server is expected to be running at `localhost:8288` (default port)
- **If the dev server is NOT running**, the connection fails with the `ECONNREFUSED` error you're seeing

### **Environment Variables Involved**

```
INNGEST_EVENT_KEY=""              # Empty — triggers dev mode
NODE_ENV=development              # Implicit (or not set) — triggers isDev:true
NUXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL="http://localhost:3000"  # Only used for public-facing URLs, not Inngest
```

### **Fix Required**

To resolve the error, you must start the Inngest dev server **before** running your app or submitting requests:

```bash
bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev
```

This command:
- Auto-discovers the `/api/inngest` endpoint (line 4 in `/server/api/inngest.ts`)
- Routes events to your local job handlers (`notifyApprovalRequested`, etc.)
- Provides a UI at `http://localhost:8288` to view event flow

### **Related Code Files (End-to-End)**

| Layer | File | Key Lines |
|-------|------|-----------|
| Frontend | `app/components/approvals/RequestOfficialDialog.vue` | 96 ($fetch call) |
| API Handler | `server/api/internal/approvals/submit.post.ts` | 23-30, 37 (error log) |
| Orchestrator | `server/utils/approvals.ts` | 96-106 (inngest.send call) |
| Inngest Client | `server/utils/inngest.ts` | 3-7 (configuration) |
| Event Handler | `server/utils/jobs/notify-approval.ts` | 18-40 (job definition) |
| Inngest Endpoint | `server/api/inngest.ts` | 1-9 (webhook handler) |
| Validators | `shared/validators/approvals.ts` | 65-77 |
| Schema | `shared/db/schema/approval.ts` | 22-72 |

### **Recent Related Commits**

- `a98062d` (2026-06-07): feat(approvals): orchestrator, endpoints, and inngest events — introduced the approval feature with Inngest events
- `7ba83e3`: feat(p10-p11): publish endpoints + Inngest client + scan/reindex jobs — initial Inngest setup
- Current branch: `main`

The feature is correctly implemented; the issue is purely operational: **the Inngest dev server needs to be running** for events to be processed in development mode.

19	export type ApprovalErrorCode =

21	  | "not_publisher_owner"

23	  | "request_not_found"

32	export class ApprovalError extends Error {
33	  readonly code: ApprovalErrorCode
34	  constructor(code: ApprovalErrorCode) {
35	    super(code)
36	    this.code = code
37	    this.name = "ApprovalError"
38	  }

41	export interface SubmitParams {
42	  extensionId: string
43	  requestedTier: OfficialTier
44	  subCat: string
45	  productLineId: string | null
46	  userId: string
47	  reason: string | undefined

50	export async function submitRequest(
51	  params: SubmitParams,
52	): Promise<approvalsRepo.ApprovalRequestRow> {
53	  // Shape rule mirrors the DB CHECK: productLineId is present iff the
54	  // requested tier is `productLine`. Surfacing both error codes lets the
55	  // endpoint give a precise message instead of bouncing the raw 23514.
56	  if (params.requestedTier === "productLine" && !params.productLineId) {
57	    throw new ApprovalError("missing_product_line")
58	  }
59	  if (params.requestedTier === "company" && params.productLineId) {
60	    throw new ApprovalError("unexpected_product_line")
61	  }
62	
63	  const db = useDb()
64	
65	  const ext = await extensionsRepo.findById(db, params.extensionId)
66	  if (!ext) throw new ApprovalError("extension_not_found")
67	  if (ext.publisherUserId !== params.userId) {
68	    throw new ApprovalError("not_publisher_owner")
69	  }
70	  if (ext.visibility !== "published") {
71	    throw new ApprovalError("extension_not_published")
72	  }
73	
74	  const row = await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
75	    // Re-check inside the transaction — race window between the visibility
76	    // load above and the insert below would otherwise let a duplicate slip
77	    // through. PG isolation is `read committed` so the second check still
78	    // sees a concurrent insert that already committed.
79	    const existing = await approvalsRepo.findPendingByExtension(
80	      tx,
81	      params.extensionId,
82	    )
83	    if (existing) throw new ApprovalError("duplicate_pending_request")
84	
85	    return approvalsRepo.insertRequest(tx, {
86	      id: crypto.randomUUID(),
87	      extensionId: params.extensionId,
88	      requestedTier: params.requestedTier,
89	      subCat: params.subCat,
90	      productLineId: params.productLineId,
91	      requestedByUserId: params.userId,
92	      reason: params.reason ?? null,
93	    })
94	  })
95	
96	  await inngest.send({
97	    name: "extension/approval.requested",
98	    data: {
99	      requestId: row.id,
100	      extensionId: row.extensionId,
101	      requestedTier: row.requestedTier,
102	      subCat: row.subCat,
103	      productLineId: row.productLineId,
104	      requesterUserId: row.requestedByUserId,
105	    },
106	  })
107	
108	  return row
109	}

111	export interface DecideParams {
112	  requestId: string
113	  action: ApprovalAction
114	  reviewerUserId: string
115	}
116	
117	export async function decideRequest(
118	  params: DecideParams,
119	): Promise<approvalsRepo.ApprovalRequestRow> {
120	  const db = useDb()
121	
122	  const current = await approvalsRepo.findById(db, params.requestId)
123	  if (!current) throw new ApprovalError("request_not_found")
124	  if (current.status !== "pending") {
125	    throw new ApprovalError("request_not_pending")
126	  }
127	
128	  // Super-admins can decide on any cell; otherwise the reviewer must be
129	  // assigned to the exact (requestedTier, subCat, productLineId?) cell this
130	  // request was filed against. Tier mismatches don't leak across cells.
131	  const allowed =
132	    (await reviewersRepo.isSuperAdmin(db, params.reviewerUserId)) ||
133	    (await approvalsRepo.isReviewerForCell(
134	      db,
135	      params.reviewerUserId,
136	      current.requestedTier,
137	      current.subCat,
138	      current.productLineId,
139	    ))
140	  if (!allowed) throw new ApprovalError("not_reviewer")
141	
142	  const outcome = decideApprovalOutcome({
143	    current: {
144	      status: current.status,
145	      requestedTier: current.requestedTier,
146	    },
147	    action: params.action,
148	    reviewerUserId: params.reviewerUserId,
149	    now: new Date(),
150	  })
151	
152	  // Optimistic locking lives inside the repository's UPDATE — if a second
153	  // reviewer decided between the pre-check above and this transaction, the
154	  // affected row count comes back zero and we surface the race as
155	  // `request_not_pending`.
156	  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
157	    const updated = await approvalsRepo.applyDecision(
158	      tx,
159	      params.requestId,
160	      outcome.request,
161	    )
162	    if (updated === 0) throw new ApprovalError("request_not_pending")
163	    if (outcome.extension) {
164	      await approvalsRepo.setExtensionOfficialTier(
165	        tx,
166	        current.extensionId,
167	        outcome.extension.officialTier,
168	        current.productLineId,
169	      )
170	    }
171	  })
172	
173	  const updated = await approvalsRepo.findById(db, params.requestId)
174	  if (!updated) throw new Error("approval request vanished mid-decide")
175	
176	  await inngest.send({
177	    name: "extension/approval.decided",
178	    data: {
179	      requestId: updated.id,
180	      extensionId: updated.extensionId,
181	      decision: outcome.request.status,
182	      decidedByUserId: params.reviewerUserId,
183	    },
184	  })
185	
186	  return updated
187	}

194	export async function withdrawRequest(
195	  params: WithdrawParams,
196	): Promise<approvalsRepo.ApprovalRequestRow> {
197	  const db = useDb()
198	
199	  const current = await approvalsRepo.findById(db, params.requestId)
200	  if (!current) throw new ApprovalError("request_not_found")
201	  if (current.requestedByUserId !== params.userId) {
202	    throw new ApprovalError("not_requester")
203	  }
204	  if (current.status !== "pending") {
205	    throw new ApprovalError("request_not_pending")
206	  }
207	
208	  const outcome = decideWithdrawOutcome({
209	    current: { status: current.status },
210	    now: new Date(),
211	  })
212	  const affected = await approvalsRepo.applyWithdraw(
213	    db,
214	    params.requestId,
215	    outcome.request.decidedAt,
216	  )
217	  if (affected === 0) throw new ApprovalError("request_not_pending")
218	
219	  const updated = await approvalsRepo.findById(db, params.requestId)
220	  if (!updated) throw new Error("approval request vanished mid-withdraw")
221	  return updated
222	}

230	export interface RevokeTierResult {

234	
235	// Super-admin Revoke action from the detail page. Loads the extension,
236	// pre-checks that it's actually Official, then atomically clears tier +
237	// productLineId and writes the audit trio. The DB-level WHERE clause in
238	// applyRevocation re-checks officialTier IS NOT NULL so a concurrent
239	// revoke / re-approval loses the race and surfaces as
240	// `extension_not_official` rather than silently clobbering the row.
241	// Caller is expected to already be a super-admin (gated at the endpoint
242	// via requireSuperAdmin) — we don't re-check here for the same reason the
243	// reviewer-assign endpoint doesn't re-check its gate.
244	export async function revokeTier(
245	  params: RevokeTierParams,
246	): Promise<RevokeTierResult> {
247	  const db = useDb()
248	  const ext = await extensionsRepo.findById(db, params.extensionId)
249	  if (!ext) throw new ApprovalError("extension_not_found")
250	  if (!ext.officialTier) throw new ApprovalError("extension_not_official")
251	
252	  const revokedAt = new Date()
253	  const affected = await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
254	    return approvalsRepo.applyRevocation(tx, params.extensionId, {
255	      revokedAt,
256	      revokedByUserId: params.superAdminUserId,
257	      revocationNote: params.note,
258	    })
259	  })
260	  if (affected === 0) throw new ApprovalError("extension_not_official")
261	
262	  await inngest.send({
263	    name: "extension/tier.revoked",
264	    data: {
265	      extensionId: params.extensionId,
266	      revokedByUserId: params.superAdminUserId,
267	      revokedAt: revokedAt.toISOString(),
268	      note: params.note,
269	    },
270	  })
271	
272	  return { extensionId: params.extensionId, revokedAt }
273	}
274	
275	export interface ReviewerQueueFilters {
276	  tier?: OfficialTier
277	  subCat?: string
278	  productLineId?: string
279	}
280	
281	// Reviewer queue — pending requests in any cell this user covers. Returns
282	// an empty array when the user has no cell assignments (and is not a
283	// super-admin); super-admins see the whole pending queue.
284	//
285	// Optional `filters` narrow the queue without changing the trust model:
286	// the cells are computed first (so a reviewer never queries outside their
287	// own assignment), then filtered, then handed to listPendingForCells. A
288	// filter that excludes every cell yields an empty `cells` array, which
289	// listPendingForCells short-circuits to [] — no special-case branch.
290	export async function listReviewerQueue(
291	  userId: string,
292	  filters: ReviewerQueueFilters = {},
293	): Promise<approvalsRepo.ApprovalRequestRow[]> {
294	  const db = useDb()
295	  let cells: Array<{
296	    tier: OfficialTier
297	    subCat: string
298	    productLineId: string | null
299	  }>
300	  if (await reviewersRepo.isSuperAdmin(db, userId)) {
301	    // Treat super-admin as "assigned to every cell". Pulled from the matrix
302	    // verbatim so an empty matrix still surfaces no rows — better than a
303	    // silent firehose. Productline IS NULL is encoded as the literal '∅' in
304	    // the dedup key so a (company, cloud, null) cell doesn't collapse onto
305	    // (productLine, cloud, '') by accident.
306	    cells = await reviewersRepo
307	      .listMatrix(db)
308	      .then((rows) =>
309	        Array.from(
310	          new Set(
311	            rows.map((r) => `${r.tier}:${r.subCat}:${r.productLineId ?? "∅"}`),
312	          ),
313	        ).map((k) => {
314	          const [tier, subCat, pl] = k.split(":") as [OfficialTier, string, string]
315	          return {
316	            tier,
317	            subCat,
318	            productLineId: pl === "∅" ? null : pl,
319	          }
320	        }),
321	      )
322	  } else {
323	    cells = await reviewersRepo.listCellsForUser(db, userId)
324	  }
325	
326	  const narrowed = cells.filter((c) => {
327	    if (filters.tier && c.tier !== filters.tier) return false
328	    if (filters.subCat && c.subCat !== filters.subCat) return false
329	    if (filters.productLineId && c.productLineId !== filters.productLineId)
330	      return false
331	    return true
332	  })
333	  return approvalsRepo.listPendingForCells(db, narrowed)
334	}
335	
336	export async function listPublisherRequests(
337	  userId: string,
338	): Promise<approvalsRepo.ApprovalRequestRow[]> {
339	  return approvalsRepo.listForPublisher(useDb(), userId)
340	}

1	import {
2	  ApprovalError,
3	  submitRequest,
4	} from "~~/server/utils/approvals"
5	import { SubmitApprovalSchema } from "~~/shared/validators/approvals"
6	
7	const ERROR_STATUS: Record<string, number> = {
8	  extension_not_found: 404,
9	  not_publisher_owner: 403,
10	  extension_not_published: 409,
11	  duplicate_pending_request: 409,
12	  missing_product_line: 400,
13	  unexpected_product_line: 400,
14	}
15	
16	export default defineEventHandler(async (event) => {
17	  const user = await requireUser(event)
18	  const body = await readValidatedBody(event, (raw) =>
19	    SubmitApprovalSchema.parse(raw),
20	  )
21	
22	  try {
23	    const row = await submitRequest({
24	      extensionId: body.extensionId,
25	      requestedTier: body.requestedTier,
26	      subCat: body.subCat,
27	      productLineId: body.productLineId ?? null,
28	      userId: user.id,
29	      reason: body.reason,
30	    })
31	    return { ok: true, request: row }
32	  } catch (err) {
33	    if (err instanceof ApprovalError) {
34	      const status = ERROR_STATUS[err.code] ?? 400
35	      throw createError({ statusCode: status, statusMessage: err.code })
36	    }
37	    console.error("internal submitRequest failed", err)
38	    throw createError({
39	      statusCode: 500,
40	      statusMessage: "approval_submit_failed",
41	    })
42	  }
43	})
44	
```

1	import { z } from "zod"
2	
3	import { inngest } from "../inngest"
4	
5	// Stub notifier for the approval workflow. v1 emits the events; this
6	// function logs them to the Inngest dev UI so the event surface is
7	// observable end-to-end without a real notification backend. Email and
8	// in-app fan-out are P1.
9	
10	const requestedSchema = z.object({
11	  requestId: z.string().min(1),
12	  extensionId: z.string().min(1),
13	  requestedTier: z.enum(["productLine", "company"]),
14	  subCat: z.string().min(1),
15	  requesterUserId: z.string().min(1),
16	})
17	
18	export const notifyApprovalRequested = inngest.createFunction(
19	  {
20	    id: "notify-approval-requested",
21	    triggers: [{ event: "extension/approval.requested" }],
22	  },
23	  async ({ event }) => {
24	    const parsed = requestedSchema.safeParse(event.data)
25	    if (!parsed.success) {
26	      console.error(
27	        "[notify-approval-requested] invalid event payload",
28	        parsed.error,
29	      )
30	      return { ok: false, reason: "invalid_event" }
31	    }
32	    // Real backend goes here. The logged shape is the contract the future
33	    // mailer will consume.
34	    console.info(
35	      "[approvals] new request",
36	      JSON.stringify({ event: "requested", ...parsed.data }),
37	    )
38	    return { ok: true }
39	  },
40	)
41	
42	const decidedSchema = z.object({
43	  requestId: z.string().min(1),
44	  extensionId: z.string().min(1),
45	  decision: z.enum(["approved", "rejected"]),
46	  decidedByUserId: z.string().min(1),
47	})
48	
49	export const notifyApprovalDecided = inngest.createFunction(
50	  {
51	    id: "notify-approval-decided",
52	    triggers: [{ event: "extension/approval.decided" }],
53	  },
54	  async ({ event }) => {
55	    const parsed = decidedSchema.safeParse(event.data)
56	    if (!parsed.success) {
57	      console.error(
58	        "[notify-approval-decided] invalid event payload",
59	        parsed.error,
60	      )
61	      return { ok: false, reason: "invalid_event" }
62	    }
63	    console.info(
64	      "[approvals] decision",
65	      JSON.stringify({ event: "decided", ...parsed.data }),
66	    )
67	    return { ok: true }
68	  },
69	)

9	const props = defineProps<{
10	  extensionId: string
11	  extensionName: string

13	}>()
14	
15	const emit = defineEmits<{
16	  (e: "submitted"): void
17	}>()

19	const { t, te, locale } = useI18n()
20	
21	const open = ref(false)
22	const tier = ref<Tier>("productLine")
23	const subCat = ref<string>(props.currentSubCat ?? "")
24	const productLineId = ref<string>("")
25	const reason = ref("")
26	const busy = ref(false)
27	const error = ref<string | null>(null)
28	
29	// Product line list is small (4 rows) and seldom changes — fetch once
30	// when the dialog opens and reuse the same ref. The endpoint requires a
31	// signed-in user, so anonymous trigger taps still work (the dialog gates
32	// on its own enclosing auth).
33	interface ProductLine {
34	  id: string
35	  labelEn: string
36	  labelZh: string
37	  sortOrder: number
38	}
39	const productLines = ref<ProductLine[]>([])
40	const productLinesLoaded = ref(false)
41	const productLinesError = ref<string | null>(null)
42	
43	async function ensureProductLines() {
44	  if (productLinesLoaded.value) return
45	  try {
46	    const res = await $fetch("/api/internal/product-lines")
47	    productLines.value = res.productLines
48	    productLinesLoaded.value = true
49	  } catch (err) {
50	    console.error("[approvals] failed to load product lines", err)
51	    productLinesError.value = t("approvals.dialog.productLinesError")
52	  }
53	}
54	
55	watch(open, (next) => {
56	  if (next) {
57	    tier.value = "productLine"
58	    subCat.value = props.currentSubCat ?? ""
59	    productLineId.value = ""
60	    reason.value = ""
61	    error.value = null
62	    productLinesError.value = null
63	    void ensureProductLines()
64	  }
65	})
66	
67	// Clear productLineId on tier change so a stale value can't ride
68	// along on a company-tier submit and trip `unexpected_product_line`.
69	watch(tier, (next) => {
70	  if (next === "company") productLineId.value = ""
71	})
72	
73	const canSubmit = computed(() => {
74	  if (busy.value) return false
75	  if (subCat.value.length === 0) return false
76	  if (tier.value === "productLine" && productLineId.value.length === 0) {
77	    return false
78	  }
79	  return true
80	})
81	
82	const orderedLines = computed(() =>
83	  [...productLines.value].sort((a, b) => a.sortOrder - b.sortOrder),
84	)
85	
86	function lineLabel(line: ProductLine): string {
87	  return locale.value === "zh" ? line.labelZh : line.labelEn
88	}
89	
90	async function handleSubmit(e: Event) {
91	  e.preventDefault()
92	  if (!canSubmit.value) return
93	  busy.value = true
94	  error.value = null
95	  try {
96	    await $fetch("/api/internal/approvals/submit", {
97	      method: "POST",
98	      body: {
99	        extensionId: props.extensionId,
100	        requestedTier: tier.value,
101	        subCat: subCat.value,
102	        productLineId:
103	          tier.value === "productLine" ? productLineId.value : undefined,
104	        reason: reason.value.trim() || undefined,
105	      },
106	    })
107	    open.value = false
108	    emit("submitted")
109	  } catch (err) {
110	    const status =
111	      err && typeof err === "object" && "statusMessage" in err
112	        ? String((err as { statusMessage: unknown }).statusMessage)
113	        : null
114	    // `t(key, fallback)` doesn't fall back — vue-i18n's second arg is
115	    // interpolation values, not a default. Probe with `te()` before
116	    // looking up the per-code message, otherwise surface the generic
117	    // copy.
118	    const key = status ? `approvals.errors.${status}` : null
119	    error.value =
120	      key && te(key) ? t(key) : t("approvals.errors.generic")
121	  } finally {
122	    busy.value = false
123	  }
124	}

docs/deploy.md:162:1. Create an Inngest app (cloud) and copy `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY` + `INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY`
docs/deploy.md:165:   https://yourdomain.com/api/inngest
docs/deploy.md:171:bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev
docs/deploy.md:173:It auto-discovers `/api/inngest` and routes events locally. No event/signing keys needed locally.
docs/deploy.md:194:| `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY` | Inngest cloud event key |
docs/api.md:3:The `/api/v1/*` surface is the contract between the web app and the CLI (or any third-party tool that wants to talk to AgentCenter). Other API routes (`/api/auth`, `/api/upload/sign`, `/api/inngest`) are implementation details and are not documented here.
docs/architecture.md:14:                └── /api/inngest            Inngest webhook
docs/architecture.md:66:│   └── inngest.ts                # webhook → inngest/node serve()
docs/architecture.md:72:    ├── inngest.ts                # Inngest client + functions
docs/architecture.md:263:   │       │  inngest.send("extension/scan.requested", { versionId, fileId })
docs/architecture.md:264:   │       │  rollback scanning→pending if inngest.send fails
docs/architecture.md:266:                                          inngest receives event,
docs/architecture.md:267:                                          calls /api/inngest webhook
docs/architecture.md:383:- Inngest client is a lazy module-level singleton in `server/utils/inngest.ts`. Function list is itself lazy-loaded via dynamic `import()` so unrelated server modules don't pull `fflate` / `smol-toml` into their import graph.
docs/architecture.md:384:- Webhook endpoint at `server/api/inngest.ts` calls `serve()` from `inngest/node` and awaits it — without the await, the Nuxt response could close before Inngest finishes signature verification + step dispatch.
docs/architecture.md:386:- For local dev, run `bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev` — auto-discovers `/api/inngest` and routes events locally. No keys needed in dev (the client uses `isDev: true` when `NODE_ENV !== "production"`).
docs/plan.md:102:│   │   ├── inngest.post.ts            # Inngest webhook handler
docs/plan.md:125:│   │   ├── inngest.ts                 # Inngest client + functions registry
docs/plan.md:176:├── inngest.config.ts
docs/plan.md:187:**API routes vs server actions**: Nuxt has no server actions. The Next.js implementation used server actions for user-initiated mutations from RSC pages (install toggle from web, save-to-collection, create collection, draft create/update). All of these become POST handlers under `server/api/internal/...` called via `$fetch` from client components. Validation lives in the handler using the same Zod schemas as before; the handler is the new trust boundary. API routes under `/api/v1/...` (public, CLI-consumed), `/api/auth/...` (Better Auth), `/api/upload/sign` (presigned URL), and `/api/inngest` (webhook) keep their original purpose.
docs/plan.md:491:| **11. Background jobs** | 1d | Inngest client + `scan-bundle` + `reindex-search` functions, webhook route at `server/api/inngest.post.ts`, status polling on publisher dashboard. Port `shared/extensions/state.ts`. |
docs/plan.md:609:| `server/utils/**` | ≥ 85% | ≥ 80% | Orchestrators over repos + helpers (auth, storage, inngest). Coverage from unit tests with mocked repos. DB client singleton excluded. |
docs/plan.md:615:Configured in `vitest.config.ts`. The `include` list deliberately scopes coverage measurement to the *currently tested* surface — files not yet in the test pyramid (`server/api/**`, `server/utils/{auth,db,storage,inngest,publish}.ts`, `server/utils/jobs/**`, `server/utils/queries/**`, `app/pages/**`, `app/components/{extension,filters,publish,ui}/**`, layout components without tests) stay outside the include so they're not silently averaged in at 0%. Add a path to `include` only after its tests actually land.
docs/plan.md:980:| `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY` | From Inngest app settings |
docs/plan.md:1000:- Local dev: `bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev` (auto-discovers `/api/inngest`).
docs/plan.md:1001:- Production: register the webhook URL `https://<origin>/api/inngest` in the Inngest dashboard.
```

8-- **Public registry API** — the `/api/v1/...` surface the CLI talks to
9:- **Background workers** — Inngest jobs scan uploaded bundles and auto-publish personal-scope extensions
10-
11-## Tech stack
12-
--
17-| Styling | Tailwind v4 |
18:| Runtime | Node 22+ (production); Bun 1.3.12 (build + scripts) |
19-| Database | PostgreSQL via Drizzle ORM (`drizzle-orm/postgres-js`) |
20:| Auth | Better Auth (cookie sessions; CLI device-code flow on the same `sessions` table) |
21-| Storage | Supabase Storage (default) or Cloudflare R2 |
22:| Background jobs | Inngest |
23-| i18n | `@nuxtjs/i18n` with `strategy: "prefix"`, locales `en` (default) + `zh` |
24-| Search | Postgres FTS + `pg_trgm` |
25-| Tests | Vitest (unit + component via `@nuxt/test-utils`) + Playwright (E2E) |
26-
27:## Quickstart
28-
29-```bash
30-# 1. Install dependencies
--
36-# 3. Migrations + sample data
37:bun run db:migrate
38:bun run db:apply-fts
39:bun run db:seed
40-
41:# 4. Dev server
42:bun run dev
43-```
44-
45:Open <http://localhost:3000>. The minimum env vars needed for local browsing are `DATABASE_URL`, `BETTER_AUTH_URL`, `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET`. Add storage + Inngest for the publish flow. See [`docs/deploy.md`](./docs/deploy.md).
46-
47-## Scripts
48-
--
50-|---|---|
51:| `bun run dev` | Dev server (Nitro + Vite) |
52:| `bun run build` | Production build → `.output/` |
53:| `bun run preview` | Run the production build locally |
54:| `bun run prepare` | Husky + `nuxt prepare` (type generation) |
55:| `bun run lint` | `@nuxt/eslint` |
56:| `bun run typecheck` | `nuxi typecheck` (vue-tsc) |

> AGENT

I have the diagnosis. The error happens because `server/utils/approvals.ts:96` calls `inngest.send(...)` and the local Inngest dev server isn't running, so the SDK's HTTP call to `localhost:8288` is refused. The request row is already committed to Postgres at that point, so the user sees "couldn't submit" even though their row was actually inserted. There's already a precedent in `server/utils/publish.ts:350-357` for catching Inngest failures — but its semantics differ (it rolls back). Let me check one design question before writing the plan.

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/.claude/plans/let-s-continue-on-the-effervescent-rocket.md",
  "content": "# Make approval Inngest sends non-fatal\n\n## Context\n\nSubmitting \"apply for official status\" surfaces *\"couldn't submit, please try again\"* to the user, with this server log:\n\n```\nERROR  internal submitRequest failed fetch failed\n[cause]: at internalConnectMultiple (node:net:1193:18)\n```\n\n**Root cause.** `submitRequest()` in `server/utils/approvals.ts:96-106` calls `inngest.send({ name: \"extension/approval.requested\", … })` *after* the row has already been committed inside the transaction at lines 74-94. In dev the Inngest client is configured with `isDev: true` (`server/utils/inngest.ts:6`) and tries to POST to the local Inngest CLI dev server (default `localhost:8288`). If that dev server isn't running, the SDK throws `fetch failed` (Node `ECONNREFUSED`); the catch in `server/api/internal/approvals/submit.post.ts:32-42` maps any non-`ApprovalError` exception to HTTP 500 `approval_submit_failed`, and the dialog (`app/components/approvals/RequestOfficialDialog.vue:109-123`) shows the generic error.\n\nThis is the wrong tradeoff for approvals:\n\n- The approval request row is the source of truth — it's already in `approvalRequests` and visible in the reviewer queue + publisher dashboard regardless of whether the event ever fires.\n- The event is consumed only by `notifyApprovalRequested` (`server/utils/jobs/notify-approval.ts:18-40`), which is a logging stub for v1. Real fan-out (email / in-app) is P1.\n- Same shape applies to the other two `inngest.send` sites in `approvals.ts` — decide (line 176) and revoke (line 262). All three are notification-style events, none have rollback semantics.\n\n`server/utils/publish.ts:350-369` already wraps `inngest.send` in try/catch, but its semantics are different: it actively rolls back the version's `status` from `scanning` back to `pending` if Inngest fails (because the scan job won't run, so the version would otherwise be stuck). Approvals have nothing to roll back — the request is legitimately submitted. So the fix is \"log and continue,\" not \"rollback.\"\n\nThe user picked option A: make Inngest non-fatal at the approvals call sites, surfaced through a small shared helper to avoid copy-pasting the try/catch three times.\n\n## Approach\n\nAdd a `safeSend` helper next to the existing `inngest` client and use it from all three sites in `server/utils/approvals.ts`. Leave `publish.ts` alone — its rollback path is intentional. Update the existing approvals unit tests to (a) keep current behavior for the happy path, (b) prove the orchestrator returns the row even when `inngest.send` rejects.\n\n### Critical files\n\n- `server/utils/inngest.ts` — add `safeSend(event)` that calls `inngest.send(event)`, catches anything thrown, and logs through `console.error(\"[inngest] safeSend failed\", { name, error })`. Returns `void`. Keep the existing `inngest` export untouched so `publish.ts` and any future caller that *wants* to fail loud can keep using it directly.\n- `server/utils/approvals.ts` — swap the three `await inngest.send(...)` calls at lines 96, 176, 262 for `await safeSend(...)`. Import name stays from `./inngest`. The transactional shape and return values do not change.\n- `server/utils/approvals.test.ts` — the existing `vi.mock(\"./inngest\", () => ({ inngest: { send: sendEvent } }))` (line 33) needs an extra `safeSend` export pointing at the same `sendEvent` spy so all current assertions on `expect(sendEvent).toHaveBeenCalledWith(...)` (e.g. line 104) keep working without re-shaping the test fixture. Add one new test under the `submitRequest` describe: when `sendEvent.mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error(\"fetch failed\"))`, `submitRequest(...)` still resolves with the inserted row and `approvalsRepo.insertRequest` was called once — i.e. the user does not see a failure for an event-only fault.\n- No schema, no validator, no API surface, no i18n change. The dialog component and the `/api/internal/approvals/submit` endpoint stay byte-for-byte the same.\n\n### Out of scope\n\n- README quickstart / Inngest dev-server documentation — user picked the code-only option. Running `bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev` is still the right thing to do locally if you want to observe events in the Inngest UI; the existing `docs/architecture.md:386` and `docs/deploy.md:171` already cover that.\n- A transactional outbox for at-least-once delivery. The current event is a stub; when real notifications land (P1) the right escalation is an outbox table + sweeper, not a synchronous failure.\n- Touching `publish.ts`. Its tolerant-with-rollback pattern is correct for the scan flow.\n\n## Verification\n\n1. `bun run test -- server/utils/approvals.test.ts` — existing assertions on `sendEvent` calls still pass; new \"resilient to Inngest failure\" test passes.\n2. `bun run typecheck` and `bun run lint`.\n3. Manual end-to-end with Inngest dev server **off**:\n   - `bun run dev` (do *not* start `bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev`).\n   - Sign in as a publisher (`SEED_PASSWORD=… bun run db:seed` first if needed); open one of your published extensions.\n   - Open the \"Apply for Official\" dialog, fill in tier + subCat (+ productLine if productLine tier), submit.\n   - **Expected:** dialog closes, parent component emits `submitted`, publisher dashboard now lists the request as pending. Server log shows `[inngest] safeSend failed { name: 'extension/approval.requested', … }` but the request was committed.\n4. Manual end-to-end with Inngest dev server **on** (regression check):\n   - Start `bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev` in a second terminal.\n   - Repeat step 3; the same UX, plus the `[approvals] new request …` log line fires from `notifyApprovalRequested`, and the event shows up in the Inngest dev UI at `http://localhost:8288`.\n5. Duplicate-submit guard still works: re-open the dialog on the same extension while a pending request exists → response should be HTTP 409 `duplicate_pending_request`, dialog shows the per-code i18n message, not the generic one.\n"
}
```

## Approved Plan:
# Make approval Inngest sends non-fatal

## Context

Submitting "apply for official status" surfaces *"couldn't submit, please try again"* to the user, with this server log:

```
ERROR  internal submitRequest failed fetch failed
[cause]: at internalConnectMultiple (node:net:1193:18)
```

**Root cause.** `submitRequest()` in `server/utils/approvals.ts:96-106` calls `inngest.send({ name: "extension/approval.requested", … })` *after* the row has already been committed inside the transaction at lines 74-94. In dev the Inngest client is configured with `isDev: true` (`server/utils/inngest.ts:6`) and tries to POST to the local Inngest CLI dev server (default `localhost:8288`). If that dev server isn't running, the SDK throws `fetch failed` (Node `ECONNREFUSED`); the catch in `server/api/internal/approvals/submit.post.ts:32-42` maps any non-`ApprovalError` exception to HTTP 500 `approval_submit_failed`, and the dialog (`app/components/approvals/RequestOfficialDialog.vue:109-123`) shows the generic error.

This is the wrong tradeoff for approvals:

- The approval request row is the source of truth — it's already in `approvalRequests` and visible in the reviewer queue + publisher dashboard regardless of whether the event ever fires.
- The event is consumed only by `notifyApprovalRequested` (`server/utils/jobs/notify-approval.ts:18-40`), which is a logging stub for v1. Real fan-out (email / in-app) is P1.
- Same shape applies to the other two `inngest.send` sites in `approvals.ts` — decide (line 176) and revoke (line 262). All three are notification-style events, none have rollback semantics.

`server/utils/publish.ts:350-369` already wraps `inngest.send` in try/catch, but its semantics are different: it actively rolls back the version's `status` from `scanning` back to `pending` if Inngest fails (because the scan job won't run, so the version would otherwise be stuck). Approvals have nothing to roll back — the request is legitimately submitted. So the fix is "log and continue," not "rollback."

The user picked option A: make Inngest non-fatal at the approvals call sites, surfaced through a small shared helper to avoid copy-pasting the try/catch three times.

## Approach

Add a `safeSend` helper next to the existing `inngest` client and use it from all three sites in `server/utils/approvals.ts`. Leave `publish.ts` alone — its rollback path is intentional. Update the existing approvals unit tests to (a) keep current behavior for the happy path, (b) prove the orchestrator returns the row even when `inngest.send` rejects.

### Critical files

- `server/utils/inngest.ts` — add `safeSend(event)` that calls `inngest.send(event)`, catches anything thrown, and logs through `console.error("[inngest] safeSend failed", { name, error })`. Returns `void`. Keep the existing `inngest` export untouched so `publish.ts` and any future caller that *wants* to fail loud can keep using it directly.
- `server/utils/approvals.ts` — swap the three `await inngest.send(...)` calls at lines 96, 176, 262 for `await safeSend(...)`. Import name stays from `./inngest`. The transactional shape and return values do not change.
- `server/utils/approvals.test.ts` — the existing `vi.mock("./inngest", () => ({ inngest: { send: sendEvent } }))` (line 33) needs an extra `safeSend` export pointing at the same `sendEvent` spy so all current assertions on `expect(sendEvent).toHaveBeenCalledWith(...)` (e.g. line 104) keep working without re-shaping the test fixture. Add one new test under the `submitRequest` describe: when `sendEvent.mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error("fetch failed"))`, `submitRequest(...)` still resolves with the inserted row and `approvalsRepo.insertRequest` was called once — i.e. the user does not see a failure for an event-only fault.
- No schema, no validator, no API surface, no i18n change. The dialog component and the `/api/internal/approvals/submit` endpoint stay byte-for-byte the same.

### Out of scope

- README quickstart / Inngest dev-server documentation — user picked the code-only option. Running `bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev` is still the right thing to do locally if you want to observe events in the Inngest UI; the existing `docs/architecture.md:386` and `docs/deploy.md:171` already cover that.
- A transactional outbox for at-least-once delivery. The current event is a stub; when real notifications land (P1) the right escalation is an outbox table + sweeper, not a synchronous failure.
- Touching `publish.ts`. Its tolerant-with-rollback pattern is correct for the scan flow.

## Verification

1. `bun run test -- server/utils/approvals.test.ts` — existing assertions on `sendEvent` calls still pass; new "resilient to Inngest failure" test passes.
2. `bun run typecheck` and `bun run lint`.
3. Manual end-to-end with Inngest dev server **off**:
   - `bun run dev` (do *not* start `bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev`).
   - Sign in as a publisher (`SEED_PASSWORD=… bun run db:seed` first if needed); open one of your published extensions.
   - Open the "Apply for Official" dialog, fill in tier + subCat (+ productLine if productLine tier), submit.
   - **Expected:** dialog closes, parent component emits `submitted`, publisher dashboard now lists the request as pending. Server log shows `[inngest] safeSend failed { name: 'extension/approval.requested', … }` but the request was committed.
4. Manual end-to-end with Inngest dev server **on** (regression check):
   - Start `bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev` in a second terminal.
   - Repeat step 3; the same UX, plus the `[approvals] new request …` log line fires from `notifyApprovalRequested`, and the event shows up in the Inngest dev UI at `http://localhost:8288`.
5. Duplicate-submit guard still works: re-open the dialog on the same extension while a pending request exists → response should be HTTP 409 `duplicate_pending_request`, dialog shows the per-code i18n message, not the generic one.

## Workflow

Per `CLAUDE.md` — feature branch, conventional commits with phase scope, no `--amend`/force-push/`--no-verify`, no `Co-Authored-By` trailer, no "Generated with Claude Code" footer in the PR body. The repo treats this as **p9 (approvals)** based on adjacent branches (`feat/p9-tier-revocation`, `feat/p9-queue-filtering`, `feat/p9-ui-admin`).

### Branch

`fix/p9-approvals-inngest-tolerance`, branched off the current `main` (`76b75a0`).

### Commits

Two natural units — the helper is a pure addition (no behavior change on its own), the fix is what's user-visible.

1. **`feat(p9-approvals): add safeSend wrapper for non-fatal inngest events`**
   - Adds `safeSend` to `server/utils/inngest.ts` next to the existing `inngest` export.
   - Logs failures with the event name + error; returns `void`.
   - No callers yet; the existing `inngest.send` export is untouched so `publish.ts`'s rollback path stays loud.

2. **`fix(p9-approvals): tolerate inngest failures in submit/decide/revoke`**
   - Swaps the three `inngest.send(...)` calls in `server/utils/approvals.ts` (lines 96, 176, 262) for `safeSend(...)`.
   - Updates `server/utils/approvals.test.ts`:
     - The `vi.mock("./inngest", …)` factory at line 33 exports `safeSend` pointing at the same `sendEvent` spy — keeps the existing `expect(sendEvent).toHaveBeenCalledWith(...)` assertions working without re-shaping them.
     - Adds one new test under the `submitRequest` describe block: when `sendEvent.mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error("fetch failed"))`, `submitRequest(...)` resolves with the row and `insertRequest` was called once.

If `bun run validate` (lint + typecheck + tests) flags coupling between the two commits I can't easily decouple, I'll collapse them into a single `fix(p9-approvals): …` commit before pushing — better than a meaningless split.

### Pre-push

`bun run validate` locally; only push once it's green. No `--no-verify`.

### PR

- Title (≤70 chars): `fix(p9-approvals): tolerate Inngest failures on apply/decide/revoke`

  "new_string": "import { Inngest } from \"inngest\"\n\nexport const inngest = new Inngest({\n  id: \"agentcenter\",\n  ...(process.env.INNGEST_EVENT_KEY ? { eventKey: process.env.INNGEST_EVENT_KEY } : {}),\n  isDev: process.env.NODE_ENV !== \"production\",\n})\n\n// Best-effort wrapper for notification-style events whose underlying write\n// has already committed. Loses the event on transport failure rather than\n// failing the user's action. Use `inngest.send` directly when the caller\n// wants the failure to surface (e.g. publish.ts's rollback path).\nexport async function safeSend(\n  event: Parameters<typeof inngest.send>[0],\n): Promise<void> {\n  try {\n    await inngest.send(event)\n  } catch (error) {\n    const name = Array.isArray(event)\n      ? event.map((e) => e.name).join(\",\")\n      : event.name\n    console.error(\"[inngest] safeSend failed\", { name, error })\n  }\n}"
}

```
diff --git a/server/utils/inngest.ts b/server/utils/inngest.ts
index 0248dd1..503f54c 100644
--- a/server/utils/inngest.ts
+++ b/server/utils/inngest.ts
@@ -6,6 +6,23 @@ export const inngest = new Inngest({
   isDev: process.env.NODE_ENV !== "production",
 })
 
+// Best-effort wrapper for notification-style events whose underlying write
+// has already committed. Loses the event on transport failure rather than
+// failing the user's action. Use `inngest.send` directly when the caller
+// wants the failure to surface (e.g. publish.ts's rollback path).
+export async function safeSend(
+  event: Parameters<typeof inngest.send>[0],
+): Promise<void> {
+  try {
+    await inngest.send(event)
+  } catch (error) {
+    const name = Array.isArray(event)
+      ? event.map((e) => e.name).join(",")
+      : event.name
+    console.error("[inngest] safeSend failed", { name, error })
+  }
+}
+
 // Lazy-loaded function refs to avoid pulling fflate / smol-toml into every
 // server module that imports the inngest client.
 let _functions: ReturnType<typeof loadFunctions> | undefined

{
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfeat(p9-approvals): add safeSend wrapper for non-fatal inngest events\n\nWraps inngest.send in a try/catch that logs but does not rethrow.\nIntended for notification-style events whose underlying write has\nalready committed, where dropping the event is preferable to failing\nthe user-facing action.\n\nThe existing `inngest` export stays untouched so call sites that *do*\nwant the failure to surface (e.g. publish.ts, which rolls back the\nversion state on send failure) keep their current loud behavior.\n\nNo callers yet — wired up in the next commit.\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit safeSend helper"
}

```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --base main --title \"fix(p9-approvals): tolerate Inngest failures on apply/decide/revoke\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\n- Submitting **Apply for Official Status** was failing with *\"couldn't submit, please try again\"* whenever the local Inngest dev server was down (`internal submitRequest failed fetch failed` in the logs). The approval row had already been committed inside the orchestrator's transaction — only the notification event was failing — so the user-facing error was misleading and the request was lost from their POV even though it existed in the queue.\n- The fix introduces a `safeSend` wrapper around `inngest.send` and uses it from the three notification-style sites in `server/utils/approvals.ts` (`submitRequest`, `decideRequest`, `revokeTier`). A transient Inngest outage now drops the event with a logged warning instead of failing the action whose write already committed.\n\n## Why this differs from `publish.ts`\n\n`server/utils/publish.ts` already wraps `inngest.send` in try/catch, but on failure it actively rolls `extensionVersions.status` back from `scanning` to `pending`. That's the right move there: if Inngest never receives the event, no scan job runs and the version would otherwise be stuck.\n\nApprovals have nothing to roll back. The request row is the source of truth and is already visible in the reviewer queue + publisher dashboard regardless of whether the event ever fires. The notification handler (`server/utils/jobs/notify-approval.ts`) is currently a logging stub for v1; real fan-out is P1, and when it lands the correct escalation is a transactional outbox + sweeper, not a synchronous failure.\n\nThe existing `inngest` export is left untouched so `publish.ts`'s rollback path keeps its current loud behavior.\n\n## Commits\n\n1. `feat(p9-approvals): add safeSend wrapper for non-fatal inngest events` — pure addition, no callers yet.\n2. `fix(p9-approvals): tolerate inngest failures in submit/decide/revoke` — swaps three call sites, updates unit-test mock factory + adds a resilience test.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `bun run validate` (lint + typecheck + 367 tests across 37 files) — green locally.\n- [x] New unit test: `submitRequest` resolves with the inserted row when the mocked `sendEvent` rejects with `\"fetch failed\"`.\n- [ ] Manual smoke (with Inngest dev server **off**): open Apply-for-Official dialog → fill tier / subCat / productLine → submit → dialog closes, publisher dashboard lists the request as pending, server log shows `[inngest] safeSend failed { name: 'extension/approval.requested', … }`.\n- [ ] Manual smoke (regression with `bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev` **on**): same UX, plus `[approvals] new request …` log line and the event visible at `http://localhost:8288`.\n- [ ] Duplicate-submit guard still surfaces HTTP 409 `duplicate_pending_request` with the per-code i18n message.\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -10",
  "description": "Open PR against main"
}
```

Perfect! The commit message explicitly says "No baseURL — the client falls back to relative /api/auth, which works on any origin without env config". This is the problem! The comment suggests that relative URLs work on the browser, but they don't work on the Node.js server during SSR.

Now let me check what the issue is with the `/api/internal/admin/me` endpoint being called on SSR. Looking back at the error, the stack says "Failed to parse URL from /api/auth/get-session". This is triggered when `require-auth` middleware calls `auth.getSession()` during SSR.

The root cause is crystal clear:

1. `createAuthClient()` is called with no `baseURL` argument
2. This defaults to relative URL paths (`/api/auth/...`)
3. On the client-side browser, this works because the browser resolves relative URLs against `window.location.origin`
4. On the server-side SSR, Node's native `fetch` cannot resolve relative URLs — it needs an absolute URL like `http://localhost:3000/api/auth/...`
5. So when `require-auth` middleware calls `auth.getSession()` during SSR, it fails

Let me now verify the full context of the approvals page flow by checking both middlewares that apply to it:

The page `/en/admin/approvals` uses:
- `require-auth` — runs on SSR and calls `auth.getSession()` (this is where the error happens)
- `require-reviewer` — also calls `$fetch("/api/internal/admin/me")` which should use absolute URL

Let me verify that the `$fetch` in `require-reviewer` does have proper headers:

Looking back at line 11 in `require-reviewer.ts`:
```typescript
const me = await $fetch("/api/internal/admin/me", {
  headers: headers as Record<string, string>,
})
```

This is using Nuxt's `$fetch`, which is smart enough to convert relative URLs to absolute during SSR. But the issue is that the `require-auth` middleware is calling `auth.getSession()` first, and that's where it fails.

Let me now create my final diagnosis.

## Summary

I've diagnosed the Nuxt 4 SSR bug on the `/en/admin/approvals` page. Here's my analysis:

### **Root Cause Location**

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/composables/useAuth.ts`  
**Line:** 6

```typescript
const _client = createAuthClient()
```

This is the single root cause. `createAuthClient()` is called with **no `baseURL` argument**, which causes it to default to relative URL paths like `/api/auth/get-session`.

### **The Bug Chain**

1. When `/en/admin/approvals` is loaded directly (or refreshed), it requires the `require-auth` middleware
2. **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/middleware/require-auth.ts`, **Lines 4-8**:
   ```typescript
   if (import.meta.server) {
     const headers = useRequestHeaders(["cookie"])
     const result = await auth.getSession({
       fetchOptions: { headers: new Headers(headers as Record<string, string>) },
     })
   ```
   During SSR, this calls `auth.getSession()` with the relative URL from the auth client
3. The Better Auth client tries to fetch from `/api/auth/get-session` as a relative URL
4. Node's native `fetch` (used during SSR) **cannot resolve relative URLs** — it requires absolute URLs like `http://localhost:3000/api/auth/get-session`
5. This throws: `Failed to parse URL from /api/auth/get-session` → 500 error

The browser normally tolerates relative URLs by resolving against `window.location.origin`, but Node does not have that capability.

### **Why The Bug Exists**

**Commit:** `cd04fed` (2026-05-12) added auth with this explicit design decision in the commit message:

> "No baseURL — the client falls back to relative /api/auth, which works on any origin without env config"

This was a reasonable choice for **client-side-only** calls (like in the browser components), but it breaks **SSR middleware calls** (like `require-auth`), where Node's `fetch` can't resolve relatives.

### **Why It Affects `/admin/approvals` Specifically**

The approvals page runs three async SSR operations via `useFetch`:
- **Lines 19-25:** `await useFetch("/api/internal/approvals/list")`
- **Lines 26-31:** `await useFetch("/api/internal/product-lines")`
- **Lines 36-46:** `await useFetch("/api/internal/admin/me")`

But the **middleware runs before the page** and hits the error first. The `require-auth` middleware is applied to every page (it's on the approvals page via `definePageMeta`), so the crash happens before the page even tries to render.

The page also applies `require-reviewer` middleware (**line 3** of the page), which uses `$fetch` (Nuxt's wrapper) instead of the auth client directly:
```typescript
const me = await $fetch("/api/internal/admin/me", ...)
```
This would work because `$fetch` is Nuxt-aware and converts relative URLs to absolute during SSR. But it never gets there because `require-auth` crashes first.

### **The Fix**

Modify `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/composables/useAuth.ts` to provide an absolute `baseURL` during SSR:

```typescript
import { createAuthClient } from "better-auth/vue"

const _client = createAuthClient({
  baseURL: import.meta.server 
    ? useRequestURL().origin 
    : undefined
})

export function useAuth() {
  return _client
}
```

**Why this works:**
- On **client-side**, `baseURL` is `undefined`, so it falls back to relative `/api/auth` (existing behavior, works fine)
- On **server-side**, `useRequestURL().origin` returns the absolute origin like `http://localhost:3000`, making the fetch absolute

### **Existing Helper Pattern in the Repo**

The repo already has a server-side pattern for getting the session without the client:

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/server/utils/auth.ts`, **Lines 80-86**:
```typescript
export async function getSessionUser(event: H3Event): Promise<SessionUser | null> {
  const auth = useAuthServer()
  const session = await auth.api.getSession({
    headers: toFetchHeaders(getRequestHeaders(event)),
  })
  return session?.user ?? null
}
```

This is the **correct** pattern for server-side session lookups — it uses the server-side auth instance (`useAuthServer()`) which doesn't rely on relative URLs. However, middleware can't use this directly because they run in the Nuxt page layer, not the server API layer.

### **Related History**

- **Commit `8e87565`** (2026-05-17): "fix(p6-auth): review follow-ups — deptId validation, ssr cleanup, useAuth note" — The comment was added noting that `useSession()` is browser-only and server callers must pass headers, but the baseURL issue wasn't addressed
- **Recent approvals work** (commits `a9bc43e` through `a575649`) all depend on SSR working, but they never hit the bug because the pages likely weren't tested with direct-load SSR (only client-side navigation)

### **Why Direct Load Triggers the Bug**

- **Client navigation** (clicking links): The middleware doesn't crash because the session is already cached from the first page load or the browser already has cookies
- **Direct page load / refresh**: Forces SSR → middleware runs → tries to call `auth.getSession()` with relative URL → crashes

This explains why the bug only appears on direct load/refresh of `/en/admin/approvals`.
```

1	type UserShape = { defaultDeptId?: string | null }

3	function toFetchHeaders(raw: Record<string, string | undefined>): Headers {
4	  const headers = new Headers()
5	  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(raw)) {
6	    if (value !== undefined) headers.append(key, value)
7	  }
8	  return headers
9	}
10	
11	export default defineNuxtRouteMiddleware(async (to) => {
12	  const auth = useAuth()
13	
14	  let rawUser: unknown = null
15	  if (import.meta.server) {
16	    const result = await auth.getSession({
17	      fetchOptions: { headers: toFetchHeaders(useRequestHeaders(["cookie"])) },
18	    })
19	    rawUser = result.data?.user ?? null
20	  } else {
21	    const session = auth.useSession()
22	    rawUser = session.value.data?.user ?? null
23	  }
24	
25	  if (!rawUser) return
26	  const user = rawUser as UserShape
27	  if (user.defaultDeptId) return
28	
29	  const localePath = useLocalePath()
30	  if (to.path === localePath("/onboard")) return
31	  return navigateTo(localePath("/onboard"))
32	})

That's why the bug only surfaces on direct-load / hard-refresh.\n\n## Approach\n\nGive the better-auth client a `baseURL` of `useRequestURL().origin` on the server, while keeping the relative-URL singleton on the client (preserves the existing reactive `useSession()` story for components like `UserButton`). SSR has no cross-request reactive sharing anyway.\n\nSketch (final code can vary slightly):\n\n```ts\nimport { createAuthClient } from \"better-auth/vue\"\n\nlet _clientClient: ReturnType<typeof createAuthClient> | undefined\n\nexport function useAuth() {\n  // Server: per-request client so Node's fetch gets an absolute URL.\n  // `useRequestURL()` reads h3's request, including x-forwarded-* headers,\n  // so previews and proxied environments resolve correctly without env config.\n  if (import.meta.server) {\n    return createAuthClient({ baseURL: useRequestURL().origin })\n  }\n  // Client: shared singleton so every consumer observes the same useSession() ref.\n  if (!_clientClient) _clientClient = createAuthClient()\n  return _clientClient\n}\n```\n\nThe existing comment (\"`useSession()` is browser-only…\") stays — it's still true. ## Approved Plan:
# Fix SSR session fetch — give better-auth client an absolute baseURL

## Context

Direct-loading or refreshing an authenticated page like `/en/admin/approvals` returns 500:

```
{ "statusCode": 500, "stack": ["Failed to parse URL from /api/auth/get-session", …] }
```

(The follow-up `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')` in the dev console is just Youch's error-page renderer choking on the bad stack frame — red herring.)

**Root cause.** `app/composables/useAuth.ts:6` builds the better-auth client with no `baseURL`:

```ts
const _client = createAuthClient()
```

That makes the client emit relative URLs like `/api/auth/get-session`. In the browser the global `fetch` resolves relatives against `window.location.origin`, so client-side calls work. During SSR, `require-auth` middleware (`app/middleware/require-auth.ts:4-8`) calls `auth.getSession({ fetchOptions: { headers } })`, which hands the relative URL to Node's native `fetch` — Node has no notion of "current origin" and throws `TypeError: Failed to parse URL from /api/auth/get-session`. The middleware's error propagates, the page never renders, and the user sees the 500 page.

The original design choice (commit `cd04fed`, *"No baseURL — the client falls back to relative `/api/auth`, which works on any origin without env config"*) was correct for client-side, but blind to the SSR middleware path that landed later in `ac39596 feat(p6-auth): require-auth and require-onboard named middleware`. The `require-onboard` middleware (`app/middleware/require-onboard.ts:15-19`) has the exact same shape, so it crashes the same way for any signed-in pre-onboard user who refreshes a page.

Client-navigation between pages doesn't hit this because the second-and-later pages render client-side (no SSR fetch). That's why the bug only surfaces on direct-load / hard-refresh.

## Approach

Give the better-auth client a `baseURL` of `useRequestURL().origin` on the server, while keeping the relative-URL singleton on the client (preserves the existing reactive `useSession()` story for components like `UserButton`). One-file change.

### The fix

`app/composables/useAuth.ts` — two lines become a small function:

- On the client: keep the existing module-level singleton. `useSession()` only matters client-side, and all components must share the same reactive ref.
- On the server: build a fresh client per `useAuth()` call with `{ baseURL: useRequestURL().origin }`. `createAuthClient` is just object construction (no I/O), so per-request cost is negligible. SSR has no cross-request reactive sharing anyway.

Sketch (final code can vary slightly):

```ts
import { createAuthClient } from "better-auth/vue"

let _clientClient: ReturnType<typeof createAuthClient> | undefined

export function useAuth() {
  // Server: per-request client so Node's fetch gets an absolute URL.
  // `useRequestURL()` reads h3's request, including x-forwarded-* headers,
  // so previews and proxied environments resolve correctly without env config.
  if (import.meta.server) {
    return createAuthClient({ baseURL: useRequestURL().origin })
  }
  // Client: shared singleton so every consumer observes the same useSession() ref.
  if (!_clientClient) _clientClient = createAuthClient()
  return _clientClient
}
```

The existing comment ("`useSession()` is browser-only…") stays — it's still true. I'll update it to also say *why* `baseURL` is set on the server, since that WHY isn't obvious from the code.

### Why this and not the alternatives

- **Set `BETTER_AUTH_URL` env var and read it in `createAuthClient`.** Brittle for preview deploys (each deploy has a different URL) and contradicts the original origin-agnostic intent. `useRequestURL()` is the idiomatic Nuxt answer.
- **Guard the SSR call sites instead of fixing the client.** Pushes the same workaround into `require-auth.ts`, `require-onboard.ts`, and every future SSR caller. Fixing the source is one diff, the rest fall into place.
- **Switch the middlewares to call the server-side helper `getSessionUser(event)` from `server/utils/auth.ts:80-86`.** Nuxt route middlewares run in the page/render layer, not the server-handler layer, so they don't have an `H3Event` in hand. Not the right shape here.

### Critical files

- `app/composables/useAuth.ts` — only file modified.
- `app/middleware/require-auth.ts` and `app/middleware/require-onboard.ts` — call sites, unchanged; they just stop crashing.
- `app/pages/[locale]/admin/approvals.vue` (and any other middleware-gated page) — unchanged; just stops 500-ing on refresh.

### Out of scope

- The Youch "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')" — a downstream effect of the 500 response shape. Once the 500 stops, Youch stops being invoked. Not a real bug to chase.
- Unit test for `useAuth.ts`. The composable is two control-flow branches around a third-party constructor; the meaningful verification is end-to-end (refresh the page, get the page, not the 500). I'd rather invest in an e2e test if recurrence ever becomes a concern.

## Verification

1. `bun run validate` — typecheck + lint + 367 existing tests still pass. No new tests proposed; see "Out of scope" above.
2. Reproduce + confirm fix manually:
   - `bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev` *not* required for this — the bug is auth, not Inngest. `bun run dev`.
   - Sign in as a reviewer / super-admin (e.g. `amy@agentcenter.dev`).
   - Navigate to `/en/admin/approvals`. **Hard-refresh** (Cmd+Shift+R / Ctrl+Shift+R). Expected: page renders normally. Today: 500.
   - Repeat from a signed-out browser → expected: redirect to `/en/sign-in?next=/en/admin/approvals`, no 500.
   - Repeat for `/en/admin/approvals/[anything]` if that nested route exists, and any other page that lists `require-auth` in `definePageMeta`.
3. Regression check for the onboarding path:
   - Sign in as a fresh user that has no `defaultDeptId` (or temporarily nullify it).
   - Hard-refresh some authenticated page. Expected: redirect to `/en/onboard` via `require-onboard`. Today: 500 inside `require-onboard` with the same `Failed to parse URL` shape.
4. Quick spot check that client-side navigation still works (the existing pattern that's not broken):
   - Click around the signed-in nav from the home page; observe no extra 500s, `UserButton`'s `useSession()` still updates after sign-out.

## Workflow

Per `CLAUDE.md` — feature branch off `main` (now at `a575649`), conventional commit with phase scope (`p6-auth` based on the file's commit history: `cd04fed feat(p6-auth)` introduced the composable, `ac39596 feat(p6-auth)` introduced the middlewares), no `--amend` / force / `--no-verify`, no `Co-Authored-By` trailer, no Claude footer in the PR body.

### Branch

`fix/p6-auth-ssr-baseurl` off `main`.

### Commits

One coherent unit — the helper change has no value split from itself.

1. **`fix(p6-auth): give better-auth client an absolute baseURL during SSR`**
   - Rewrites `app/composables/useAuth.ts` to return a per-request server client with `useRequestURL().origin` and keep the client-side singleton.
   - Updates the leading comment to explain the WHY of the SSR branch.

### Pre-push

`bun run validate` locally; only push once green. No `--no-verify`.

### PR

- Title (≤70 chars): `fix(p6-auth): give better-auth client an absolute baseURL during SSR`
- Body sections: **Summary** (the 500 + root cause in two bullets), **Why this shape** (per-request server client vs. env var vs. middleware-side guards), **Test plan** (the four-step verification list above).
- No agent-attribution noise per `CLAUDE.md`.
- I'll open the PR with `gh pr create --base main` and surface the URL back; you merge.

The\n// browser resolves better-auth's default relative URLs against\n// `window.location.origin`; Node's native `fetch` does not, and would throw\n// `Failed to parse URL from /api/auth/get-session` the moment any SSR\n// middleware (require-auth, require-onboard) tries to read the session.\n// `useRequestURL()` reads h3's request including x-forwarded-* headers, so\n// preview and proxied environments resolve correctly without env config.\n\nlet _clientSingleton: ReturnType<typeof createAuthClient> | undefined\n\nexport function useAuth() {\n  if (import.meta.server) {\n    return createAuthClient({ baseURL: useRequestURL().origin })\n  }\n  if (!_clientSingleton) _clientSingleton = createAuthClient()\n  return _clientSingleton\n}\n"

{
  "command": "git add app/composables/useAuth.ts && git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(p6-auth): give better-auth client an absolute baseURL during SSR\n\nDirect-loading or refreshing an authenticated page like\n/en/admin/approvals returned 500 with\n`Failed to parse URL from /api/auth/get-session`.\n\nThe better-auth client was constructed without `baseURL`, so it emits\nrelative URLs like /api/auth/get-session. In the browser the global\nfetch resolves relatives against window.location.origin and everything\nworks. During SSR, require-auth (and require-onboard) call\nauth.getSession({ fetchOptions: { headers } }), which hands the\nrelative URL straight to Node's native fetch — Node has no notion of\n\"current origin\" and throws TypeError, the middleware bubbles the\nerror, and the page is replaced by the 500 renderer. (The Youch\n\"Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')\" follow-up\nin the dev console is just the error-page renderer choking on the bad\nstack frame, not a second bug.)\n\nMove the construction into the composable body. On the server, build\na fresh client per call with { baseURL: useRequestURL().origin } so\nNode sees an absolute URL — useRequestURL() reads h3's request\nincluding x-forwarded-* headers, so previews and proxied environments\nwork without env config (matching the origin-agnostic intent of the\noriginal `cd04fed`). On the client, keep a module-level singleton so\nevery component observes the same useSession() reactive ref.\n\nThe call sites — app/middleware/require-auth.ts and require-onboard.ts\n— do not change.\nEOF\n)\"",
  "description": "Commit the SSR baseURL fix"
}

```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --base main --title \"fix(p6-auth): give better-auth client an absolute baseURL during SSR\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\n- Direct-loading or refreshing an authenticated page (e.g. `/en/admin/approvals`) returned **500** with `Failed to parse URL from /api/auth/get-session`. The Youch `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')` in the dev console after that is just the error-page renderer choking on the bad stack frame — same single bug.\n- The better-auth client was constructed without `baseURL` (`app/composables/useAuth.ts:6`), so it emitted relative URLs. In the browser that works because `fetch` resolves against `window.location.origin`; during SSR, `require-auth` and `require-onboard` middlewares call `auth.getSession({ fetchOptions: { headers } })`, Node's native `fetch` rejects the relative URL, and the page never renders. Client-side navigation between pages didn't trip it (no SSR on the second nav), which is why it only showed up on hard-refresh.\n\n## Why this shape\n\n- Build a fresh better-auth client per `useAuth()` call **on the server** with `{ baseURL: useRequestURL().origin }`. `createAuthClient` is cheap (no I/O), and SSR has no cross-request reactive state to preserve.\n- Keep a module-level **singleton on the client** so every component observes the same `useSession()` reactive ref.\n- `useRequestURL()` honors `x-forwarded-*` headers via h3, so previews and proxied environments work without env config — keeping the origin-agnostic intent of the original commit (`cd04fed`).\n\nAlternatives considered & rejected:\n\n- **`BETTER_AUTH_URL` env var.** Brittle across preview deploys; each gets a different URL.\n- **Guard the SSR call sites in each middleware.** Pushes the workaround into every current and future SSR caller; fixing the composable is one diff.\n- **Switch middlewares to the server-side helper `getSessionUser(event)` (`server/utils/auth.ts:80`).** Nuxt route middlewares don't hold an `H3Event`; wrong shape here.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `bun run validate` — lint + typecheck + 367/367 tests green locally.\n- [ ] Manual: sign in (e.g. `amy@agentcenter.dev`), navigate to `/en/admin/approvals`, **hard-refresh** — expected the page renders; before this fix it 500s.\n- [ ] Manual: hard-refresh `/en/admin/approvals` while signed out — expected redirect to `/en/sign-in?next=/en/admin/approvals`, no 500.\n- [ ] Manual: sign in as a fresh user with `defaultDeptId = null`, hard-refresh any authenticated page — expected redirect to `/en/onboard` via `require-onboard` (currently 500s with the same shape).\n- [ ] Manual: spot check that client-side nav still works — `UserButton`'s `useSession()` updates after sign-in/out.\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Open PR"
}
```

109	// Cell-aware authorisation for matrix edits. Encodes the delegation rule
110	// from ADR-0001 (2026-06-08 addendum):
111	//   - super-admins can edit every cell;
112	//   - company-tier admins of subCat X may manage productLine cells of
113	//     subCat X (any productLine);
114	//   - company-tier cells stay super-admin-only.
115	// Callers pass the cell coordinates explicitly — for `unassign`, load the
116	// target row first so the gate authorises against that row's coordinates
117	// rather than the caller's request body.
118	export async function requireCellAdmin(
119	  event: H3Event,
120	  cell: { tier: OfficialTier; subCat: string; productLineId: string | null },
121	): Promise<SessionUser> {
122	  const user = await requireUser(event)
123	  const db = useDb()
124	  if (await isSuperAdmin(db, user.id)) return user
125	  if (
126	    cell.tier === "productLine" &&
127	    (await isCompanyAdminForSubCat(db, user.id, cell.subCat))

130	  }

> AGENT

The fix turned the 500 into "session not found on refresh." That means SSR is no longer crashing on URL parse, but the better-auth self-call from middleware isn't recovering the user — almost certainly because cookies aren't surviving the server-to-self HTTP roundtrip, or better-auth's client doesn't honor `fetchOptions.headers` the way the call site assumed. Let me investigate before patching blindly.

function getAtomKey(str) {
	return `use${capitalizeFirstLetter(str)}`;
}
function createAuthClient(options) {
	const { baseURL, pluginPathMethods, pluginsActions, pluginsAtoms, $fetch, $store, atomListeners } = getClientConfig(options, false);
	const resolvedHooks = {};
	for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(pluginsAtoms)) resolvedHooks[getAtomKey(key)] = () => useStore(value);
	function useSession(useFetch) {

				return {
					data: res.data,
					isPending: false,
					error: res.error
				};
			});
		}
		return resolvedHooks.useSession();
	}
	return createDynamicPathProxy({
		...pluginsActions,
		...resolvedHooks,
		useSession,
		$fetch,
		$store
	}, $fetch, pluginPathMethods, pluginsAtoms, atomListeners);
}

> AGENT

Diagnosis: The baseURL fix lets the SSR call complete instead of throwing on URL parse, but it now does a **real HTTP loopback** to `http://localhost:3000/api/auth/get-session`. That self-call doesn't recover the session — either the Cookie header is being stripped by Node's fetch / better-fetch on the way out, or better-auth's handler doesn't trust the loopback request. Either way, the design is wrong: SSR shouldn't make a real network round-trip to its own server.

The codebase already has the right pattern in `app/middleware/require-reviewer.ts:11-13` — use `$fetch` with the cookie header. During SSR, Nuxt's `$fetch` short-circuits to Nitro's in-process handler dispatch (no real network), and cookies pass through cleanly.

**Fix:** switch the SSR branches of `require-auth` and `require-onboard` from the better-auth client to `$fetch("/api/auth/get-session", { headers })`. Keep the baseURL change in `useAuth.ts` as a defensive guard for any future SSR caller. Extend PR #50 with the additional commit and update title/body so the PR actually delivers a working refresh.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/middleware/require-auth.ts",
  "content": "// SSR session lookup goes through `$fetch` to better-auth's own\n// `/api/auth/get-session` route, not the better-auth Vue client. During\n// SSR Nuxt's `$fetch` short-circuits to Nitro's in-process handler\n// dispatch (no real network), so the cookie we forward via headers\n// reaches the auth handler exactly as a browser request would. The client\n// alternative used a real HTTP loopback whose cookie/origin handling was\n// unreliable — same pattern as `app/middleware/require-reviewer.ts`.\n\ntype SessionResponse = { user?: { id: string } | null } | null\n\nexport default defineNuxtRouteMiddleware(async (to) => {\n  let hasUser = false\n\n  if (import.meta.server) {\n    const headers = useRequestHeaders([\"cookie\"])\n    try {\n      const session = await $fetch<SessionResponse>(\"/api/auth/get-session\", {\n        headers: headers as Record<string, string>,\n      })\n      hasUser = !!session?.user\n    } catch {\n      hasUser = false\n    }\n  } else {\n    const session = useAuth().useSession()\n    hasUser = !!session.value.data?.user\n  }\n\n  if (!hasUser) {\n    const localePath = useLocalePath()\n    return navigateTo({\n      path: localePath(\"/sign-in\"),\n      query: { next: to.fullPath },\n    })\n  }\n})\n"
}

{
  "command": "gh pr edit 50 --title \"fix(p6-auth): fix SSR session lookup on direct page load / refresh\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nHard-refreshing an authenticated page like `/en/admin/approvals` was first 500-ing (`Failed to parse URL from /api/auth/get-session`), and after the first commit landed locally it stopped 500-ing but instead **bounced the user back to `/sign-in`** — even with a valid session cookie. Two coupled fixes ship together so PR #50 actually delivers a working refresh:\n\n1. **`app/composables/useAuth.ts`** — give better-auth's client an absolute `baseURL` derived from `useRequestURL().origin` during SSR. Without it, the relative `/api/auth/get-session` URL crashed Node's native `fetch` the moment any SSR middleware called `auth.getSession(…)`. Kept as defensive coverage even though no middleware uses the client on the server anymore — `UserButton.vue` still calls `useAuth()` during SSR for its reactive `useSession()` ref, and any future SSR caller is protected.\n2. **`app/middleware/require-auth.ts` + `app/middleware/require-onboard.ts`** — switch the SSR session lookup from the better-auth client to `$fetch(\"/api/auth/get-session\", { headers: useRequestHeaders([\"cookie\"]) })`. During SSR `$fetch` short-circuits to Nitro's in-process handler dispatch — no real network call — so the cookie reaches better-auth's handler exactly as a browser request would, and the session validates. This is the same pattern `app/middleware/require-reviewer.ts` has been using.\n\n## Why `$fetch` and not the auth client\n\nAfter (1), the better-auth client *did* complete the call, but as a real HTTP loopback (`http://localhost:3000/api/auth/get-session`). That round-trip didn't recover the session — most likely the cookie wasn't surviving the cross-stack hop, or better-auth's handler treated the self-loopback as untrusted. Rather than debug a stack we shouldn't be using in the first place, route SSR through Nitro's local dispatcher directly, which has well-defined cookie semantics and zero network cost.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `bun run validate` — lint + typecheck + 367/367 tests still green.\n- [ ] Manual: sign in (`amy@agentcenter.dev`), navigate to `/en/admin/approvals`, **hard-refresh** — the page should render normally, not 500 and not bounce to `/sign-in`.\n- [ ] Manual: hard-refresh `/en/admin/approvals` while signed out — should redirect to `/en/sign-in?next=/en/admin/approvals`.\n- [ ] Manual: sign in as a user with `defaultDeptId = null`, hard-refresh any authenticated page — should redirect to `/en/onboard` via `require-onboard` (was 500 before commit 1, was a `/sign-in` bounce after commit 1 alone).\n- [ ] Manual: spot check client-side nav — `UserButton`'s `useSession()` still updates after sign-in / sign-out.\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Retitle and rewrite PR #50 body"
}
