```
Perfect! I found the key documentation at line 195:

```
SSR branch:  auth.getSession({ headers: toFetchHeaders(useRequestHeaders(["cookie"])) })
```

This shows that the proper way to handle SSR session lookup is using `toFetchHeaders()` to convert the headers. But the current middleware code is just casting to `Record<string, string>` without proper conversion.

Now I have the full picture. Let me summarize my findings:

## Root Cause Analysis

The bug is in how `app/middleware/require-auth.ts` and `app/middleware/require-onboard.ts` handle headers when calling `$fetch` during SSR.

**The Problem:**
The middleware code does:
```typescript
const headers = useRequestHeaders(["cookie"])
const session = await $fetch<SessionResponse>("/api/auth/get-session", {
  headers: headers as Record<string, string>,
})
```

This casts the headers directly without proper conversion. When `useRequestHeaders` returns a header value that is an array (which can happen with the `cookie` header in some Nuxt versions), the cast `as Record<string, string>` silently loses the array value, and the cookie header never reaches better-auth's handler.

**Why it works on first load but fails on refresh:** The first SSR render might catch the cookie if the request context is preserved differently, but on a browser refresh (which is a new independent HTTP request through Nuxt's full SSR pipeline), the cookie array conversion issue manifests.

**The Solution:**
The middleware should use the same `toFetchHeaders()` helper that already exists in `server/utils/auth.ts` to properly convert header objects that may contain array values. This function correctly handles multi-valued headers like cookies.

However, there's a deeper architectural issue: The middleware is running in the Nuxt/Nitro app context, and calling `$fetch("/api/auth/get-session", ...)` might not actually be the best pattern. The more robust approach would be to either:

1. **Use `useRequestFetch()`** - Nuxt's context-aware fetch that preserves the request context and headers automatically during SSR
2. **Call `getSessionUser(event)` server-side directly** - Since middleware can access `useRequestEvent()` to get the h3 event

The architecture doc confirms that `require-auth` should use the server-side `getSessionUser` pattern shown in `server/utils/auth.ts`, not route through HTTP.

---

## Report

**Single most likely root cause:**
The middleware is calling `$fetch` with headers that aren't properly converted. When `useRequestHeaders(["cookie"])` returns the cookie header value as an array (rather than a string), the `as Record<string, string>` cast causes the header value to be lost during the `$fetch` call, so the auth handler never receives the session cookie.

**Minimal fix:**
Import and use the existing `toFetchHeaders()` helper from `server/utils/auth.ts` in both middleware files:

```typescript
// app/middleware/require-auth.ts
import { toFetchHeaders } from "~/server/utils/auth"

// In the SSR branch:
const headers = useRequestHeaders(["cookie"])
const session = await $fetch<SessionResponse>("/api/auth/get-session", {
  headers: toFetchHeaders(headers),
})
```

**Better architectural fix:**
Instead of calling `$fetch`, use `useRequestFetch()` (Nuxt's context-aware fetch) OR use `useRequestEvent()` to get the h3 event and call `getSessionUser(event)` directly from the server util, which is what `require-reviewer.ts` / `require-super-admin.ts` should also probably be doing for consistency.

**Existing helper to reuse:**
`server/utils/auth.ts` already exports `toFetchHeaders()` and `getSessionUser()` which do exactly what the middleware needs.
```

1	import {

4	} from "~~/server/repositories/reviewers"
5	
6	// Lightweight "what can I do in the admin surface" probe — backs the
7	// require-reviewer / require-super-admin client middleware so the pages
8	// can fail fast with 403 → redirect rather than rendering and then
9	// hitting an empty page.
10	//
11	// Returns safe defaults for anonymous callers rather than 401, because
12	// the detail page renders for anonymous browsers too and needs to read
13	// this endpoint without bailing out — the gates on the actual admin
14	// pages still throw 401 separately. `cells` is returned alongside the
15	// role booleans so the approvals queue page can render smart pickers
16	// bounded to what the viewer actually covers; an empty array means "no
17	// cells" rather than "any cell allowed".
18	export default defineEventHandler(async (event) => {
19	  const user = await getSessionUser(event)
20	  if (!user) {
21	    return { isSuperAdmin: false, isReviewer: false, cells: [] }
22	  }
23	  const db = useDb()
24	  const [superAdmin, cells] = await Promise.all([
25	    isSuperAdmin(db, user.id),
26	    listCellsForUser(db, user.id),
27	  ])
28	  return {
29	    isSuperAdmin: superAdmin,
30	    isReviewer: superAdmin || cells.length > 0,
31	    cells,
32	  }
33	})

> AGENT

I traced the Nitro internals. Critical finding: `useRequestFetch()` returns `event.$fetch` which calls `fetchWithEvent` (h3) — that helper auto-forwards `Cookie` (it's not in h3's ignore list) plus the rest of the original request headers. Plain `$fetch` does NOT go through `fetchWithEvent`; it goes through `localFetch`, and whether explicit `headers: { cookie }` survives that path is at minimum non-obvious and apparently broken in practice for the user.

Even better: avoid the better-auth handler entirely on SSR. The repo already has `getSessionUser(event)` (`server/utils/auth.ts:80`) that reads the session directly server-side. I'll add a tiny internal endpoint that exposes it and have middleware hit that via `useRequestFetch()` — same pattern `require-reviewer.ts` uses (call internal endpoint, get JSON).

Writing the plan.

> TOOL

tool_use Write
id: toolu_019m4pELVEAzXoUN6FcLKUcD
```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/.claude/plans/let-s-continue-on-the-effervescent-rocket.md",
  "content": "# Make SSR session lookup actually work — route through useRequestFetch + a thin internal endpoint\n\n## Context\n\nPR #50 already landed two commits on `fix/p6-auth-ssr-baseurl`:\n\n1. `38a2115` — `app/composables/useAuth.ts` gives better-auth's Vue client an absolute `baseURL` so Node's native `fetch` stops throwing `Failed to parse URL from /api/auth/get-session` during SSR.\n2. `b44997d` — `app/middleware/require-auth.ts` + `app/middleware/require-onboard.ts` were switched off the better-auth client to plain `$fetch(\"/api/auth/get-session\", { headers: useRequestHeaders([\"cookie\"]) })`, on the assumption that the cookie would flow through Nitro's in-process dispatch to better-auth's handler.\n\nIt doesn't. The user reports that hard-refreshing any authenticated page (now `/en/publish` — `app/pages/publish/index.vue:4` declares `middleware: [\"require-auth\", \"require-onboard\"]`) still bounces them to `/sign-in`. So whatever cookie path commit `b44997d` chose isn't reaching better-auth's get-session handler — either the cookie is dropped on the way through `globalThis.$fetch → localFetch → fetchNodeRequestHandler`, or better-auth's own handler rejects the in-process request for some other reason (CSRF / origin check, cookie-cache lookup keyed on something request-shape-specific). Either way the design is wrong: SSR session lookup is rebouncing through better-auth's HTTP surface when it has no business doing that.\n\nWhile digging I found two things that point cleanly at the right shape:\n\n- **`useRequestFetch()`** (in `node_modules/nuxt/dist/app/composables/ssr.js:39-44`) returns `event.$fetch` on the server, which is a `fetchWithEvent` wrapper from h3 (`node_modules/h3/dist/index.mjs:1263`). That wrapper merges `getProxyRequestHeaders(event)` into the outgoing request — `cookie` is not in h3's ignored-headers list (line 1138), so cookies are forwarded automatically. This is the Nuxt-documented way to make a cookie-forwarding SSR fetch, and we are *not* using it.\n- **`getSessionUser(event)`** (in `server/utils/auth.ts:80-86`) already does a direct server-side session read via `useAuthServer().api.getSession({ headers })`. No HTTP roundtrip, no better-auth handler. This is the same helper the existing `server/api/internal/admin/me.get.ts:19` uses, which `require-reviewer.ts` already exercises successfully.\n\nThe fix is to combine those two: add a thin internal `me` endpoint that returns `{ user }`, and have the middlewares hit it via `useRequestFetch()`. The baseURL fix from commit 1 stays in `useAuth.ts` as defensive coverage for any future SSR caller that still touches the better-auth Vue client (today only `UserButton.vue`'s reactive `useSession()`, which doesn't fetch).\n\n## Approach\n\n### New endpoint\n\n`server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts` — lightweight handler that wraps `getSessionUser(event)`:\n\n```ts\nexport default defineEventHandler(async (event) => {\n  const user = await getSessionUser(event)\n  return { user: user ?? null }\n})\n```\n\nAnonymous → `{ user: null }`. Returning a flat object lets middlewares branch on `result.user`. Type derives from `SessionUser` exported by `server/utils/auth.ts:69` so the middleware response typing stays accurate without redeclaration.\n\n### Middleware switch\n\nFor each of `app/middleware/require-auth.ts` and `app/middleware/require-onboard.ts`:\n\n```ts\nif (import.meta.server) {\n  try {\n    const { user } = await useRequestFetch()<{ user: { id: string; defaultDeptId?: string | null } | null }>(\n      \"/api/internal/auth/me\",\n    )\n    // …branch on user\n  } catch {\n    // treat as anon, redirect-to-sign-in below\n  }\n} else {\n  const session = useAuth().useSession()\n  // …existing client branch\n}\n```\n\nThe client branches stay identical — they still drive `UserButton`'s reactive session through better-auth's Vue store.\n\nDrop the local `toFetchHeaders` helper from `require-onboard.ts` and the `useRequestHeaders([\"cookie\"])` calls from both — `useRequestFetch()` forwards them automatically, the explicit plumbing is no longer needed.\n\n### Why this and not the alternatives\n\n- **Keep plain `$fetch` + manually-pinned `headers: useRequestHeaders([\"cookie\"])`.** This is what commit `b44997d` shipped and what the user is currently looking at; it does not work on their machine. Whether the failure is in Nitro's `localFetch`, in `fetchNodeRequestHandler`, or in better-auth's handler treating in-process requests as untrusted is academic — the pattern is fragile by construction. `useRequestFetch` is the documented Nuxt idiom.\n- **Hit `/api/auth/get-session` via `useRequestFetch()` directly.** Tempting (it's one fewer file), but it still routes through better-auth's handler, which I have no evidence handles in-process dispatch cleanly — same risk surface. Going through our own endpoint that calls `getSessionUser(event)` is a known-good path (it's exactly what `/api/internal/admin/me` already does in production).\n- **Import `server/utils/auth.ts` into the middleware directly via `useRequestEvent()`.** Would break the client bundle — `server/utils/auth.ts` pulls in `better-auth`, the Drizzle adapter, and `pg`. Not viable.\n- **Revert commit 1's baseURL fix.** No SSR call now needs the absolute baseURL after this lands, but `UserButton.vue` still calls `useAuth()` during SSR. Yanking the SSR branch risks any future caller bringing back the original 500 the moment they touch `auth.getSession()` from a Vue component during SSR. The defensive cost is six lines. Keep it.\n\n### Out of scope\n\n- `require-reviewer.ts` and `require-super-admin.ts`. Both use the same plain-`$fetch`-with-explicit-headers pattern, and would silently bounce reviewers to `/` instead of throwing — so the same bug class probably affects them on refresh. But the user's reported path is `/en/publish`, which doesn't run either of them, and switching all four middlewares in one PR widens the blast radius unnecessarily. If the publish fix exposes the same bug on `/admin/*`, mirror the pattern in a follow-up PR with the same diff shape.\n- New unit test for `server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts`. The handler is a one-line wrapper over a fully-tested helper (`server/utils/auth.test.ts` exercises `getSessionUser`); a duplicate test would only confirm DI. The real verification here is manual-refresh, see below.\n\n## Critical files\n\n- **new** `server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts` — thin handler returning `{ user }`.\n- `app/middleware/require-auth.ts` — SSR branch switches to `useRequestFetch()(\"/api/internal/auth/me\")`.\n- `app/middleware/require-onboard.ts` — same; drop the obsolete `toFetchHeaders` helper at the top of the file.\n- *(unchanged but worth naming)* `server/utils/auth.ts:80-86` (`getSessionUser`), `app/composables/useAuth.ts` (baseURL stays).\n\n## Verification\n\n1. `bun run validate` — lint + typecheck + 367 existing tests still pass. No new tests proposed (see Out of scope).\n2. Reproduce + confirm the fix manually against the user's exact scenario:\n   - `bun run dev` (Inngest dev server not needed for this path).\n   - Sign in as `amy@agentcenter.dev` (or any seeded publisher).\n   - Navigate to `/en/publish`. ## Approved Plan:
# Make SSR session lookup actually work — route through useRequestFetch + a thin internal endpoint

## Context

PR #50 already landed two commits on `fix/p6-auth-ssr-baseurl`:

1. `38a2115` — `app/composables/useAuth.ts` gives better-auth's Vue client an absolute `baseURL` so Node's native `fetch` stops throwing `Failed to parse URL from /api/auth/get-session` during SSR.
2. `b44997d` — `app/middleware/require-auth.ts` + `app/middleware/require-onboard.ts` were switched off the better-auth client to plain `$fetch("/api/auth/get-session", { headers: useRequestHeaders(["cookie"]) })`, on the assumption that the cookie would flow through Nitro's in-process dispatch to better-auth's handler.

It doesn't. The user reports that hard-refreshing any authenticated page (now `/en/publish` — `app/pages/publish/index.vue:4` declares `middleware: ["require-auth", "require-onboard"]`) still bounces them to `/sign-in`. So whatever cookie path commit `b44997d` chose isn't reaching better-auth's get-session handler — either the cookie is dropped on the way through `globalThis.$fetch → localFetch → fetchNodeRequestHandler`, or better-auth's own handler rejects the in-process request for some other reason (CSRF / origin check, cookie-cache lookup keyed on something request-shape-specific). Either way the design is wrong: SSR session lookup is rebouncing through better-auth's HTTP surface when it has no business doing that.

While digging I found two things that point cleanly at the right shape:

- **`useRequestFetch()`** (in `node_modules/nuxt/dist/app/composables/ssr.js:39-44`) returns `event.$fetch` on the server, which is a `fetchWithEvent` wrapper from h3 (`node_modules/h3/dist/index.mjs:1263`). That wrapper merges `getProxyRequestHeaders(event)` into the outgoing request — `cookie` is not in h3's ignored-headers list (line 1138), so cookies are forwarded automatically. This is the Nuxt-documented way to make a cookie-forwarding SSR fetch, and we are *not* using it.
- **`getSessionUser(event)`** (in `server/utils/auth.ts:80-86`) already does a direct server-side session read via `useAuthServer().api.getSession({ headers })`. No HTTP roundtrip, no better-auth handler. This is the same helper the existing `server/api/internal/admin/me.get.ts:19` uses, which `require-reviewer.ts` already exercises successfully.

The fix is to combine those two: add a thin internal `me` endpoint that returns `{ user }`, and have the middlewares hit it via `useRequestFetch()`. The baseURL fix from commit 1 stays in `useAuth.ts` as defensive coverage for any future SSR caller that still touches the better-auth Vue client (today only `UserButton.vue`'s reactive `useSession()`, which doesn't fetch).

## Approach

### New endpoint

`server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts` — lightweight handler that wraps `getSessionUser(event)`:

```ts
export default defineEventHandler(async (event) => {
  const user = await getSessionUser(event)
  return { user: user ?? null }
})
```

Anonymous → `{ user: null }`. Returning a flat object lets middlewares branch on `result.user`. Type derives from `SessionUser` exported by `server/utils/auth.ts:69` so the middleware response typing stays accurate without redeclaration.

### Middleware switch

For each of `app/middleware/require-auth.ts` and `app/middleware/require-onboard.ts`:

```ts
if (import.meta.server) {
  try {
    const { user } = await useRequestFetch()<{ user: { id: string; defaultDeptId?: string | null } | null }>(
      "/api/internal/auth/me",
    )
    // …branch on user
  } catch {
    // treat as anon, redirect-to-sign-in below
  }
} else {
  const session = useAuth().useSession()
  // …existing client branch
}
```

The client branches stay identical — they still drive `UserButton`'s reactive session through better-auth's Vue store.

Drop the local `toFetchHeaders` helper from `require-onboard.ts` and the `useRequestHeaders(["cookie"])` calls from both — `useRequestFetch()` forwards them automatically, the explicit plumbing is no longer needed.

### Why this and not the alternatives

- **Keep plain `$fetch` + manually-pinned `headers: useRequestHeaders(["cookie"])`.** This is what commit `b44997d` shipped and what the user is currently looking at; it does not work on their machine. Whether the failure is in Nitro's `localFetch`, in `fetchNodeRequestHandler`, or in better-auth's handler treating in-process requests as untrusted is academic — the pattern is fragile by construction. `useRequestFetch` is the documented Nuxt idiom.
- **Hit `/api/auth/get-session` via `useRequestFetch()` directly.** Tempting (it's one fewer file), but it still routes through better-auth's handler, which I have no evidence handles in-process dispatch cleanly — same risk surface. Going through our own endpoint that calls `getSessionUser(event)` is a known-good path (it's exactly what `/api/internal/admin/me` already does in production).
- **Import `server/utils/auth.ts` into the middleware directly via `useRequestEvent()`.** Would break the client bundle — `server/utils/auth.ts` pulls in `better-auth`, the Drizzle adapter, and `pg`. Not viable.
- **Revert commit 1's baseURL fix.** No SSR call now needs the absolute baseURL after this lands, but `UserButton.vue` still calls `useAuth()` during SSR. Yanking the SSR branch risks any future caller bringing back the original 500 the moment they touch `auth.getSession()` from a Vue component during SSR. The defensive cost is six lines. Keep it.

### Out of scope

- `require-reviewer.ts` and `require-super-admin.ts`. Both use the same plain-`$fetch`-with-explicit-headers pattern, and would silently bounce reviewers to `/` instead of throwing — so the same bug class probably affects them on refresh. But the user's reported path is `/en/publish`, which doesn't run either of them, and switching all four middlewares in one PR widens the blast radius unnecessarily. If the publish fix exposes the same bug on `/admin/*`, mirror the pattern in a follow-up PR with the same diff shape.
- New unit test for `server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts`. The handler is a one-line wrapper over a fully-tested helper (`server/utils/auth.test.ts` exercises `getSessionUser`); a duplicate test would only confirm DI. The real verification here is manual-refresh, see below.

## Critical files

- **new** `server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts` — thin handler returning `{ user }`.
- `app/middleware/require-auth.ts` — SSR branch switches to `useRequestFetch()("/api/internal/auth/me")`.
- `app/middleware/require-onboard.ts` — same; drop the obsolete `toFetchHeaders` helper at the top of the file.
- *(unchanged but worth naming)* `server/utils/auth.ts:80-86` (`getSessionUser`), `app/composables/useAuth.ts` (baseURL stays).

## Verification

1. `bun run validate` — lint + typecheck + 367 existing tests still pass. No new tests proposed (see Out of scope).
2. Reproduce + confirm the fix manually against the user's exact scenario:
   - `bun run dev` (Inngest dev server not needed for this path).
   - Sign in as `amy@agentcenter.dev` (or any seeded publisher).
   - Navigate to `/en/publish`. Hard-refresh. **Expected:** page renders. **Today:** bounces to `/en/sign-in`.
   - Repeat hard-refresh on `/en/admin/approvals` (covers `require-reviewer`'s downstream effect — they should still be in, since `require-auth` now actually returns true).
   - Sign out, hard-refresh `/en/publish` — should redirect to `/en/sign-in?next=/en/publish`, no 500.
3. Onboarding regression check:
   - Sign in as a user with `defaultDeptId = null` (or null one out for testing).
   - Hard-refresh any authenticated page. **Expected:** `/en/onboard`.
4. Spot check that `UserButton`'s reactive `useSession()` still updates after sign-in / sign-out on the client — i.e. the baseURL fix in `useAuth.ts` hasn't been disturbed.

## Workflow

Per `CLAUDE.md` — same feature branch (`fix/p6-auth-ssr-baseurl`), no `--amend` once pushed, no force-push, no `--no-verify`, no `Co-Authored-By` trailer, no Claude footer in the PR body.

### Branch

Stay on `fix/p6-auth-ssr-baseurl`. Add a third commit on top of `b44997d`.

### Commits

One coherent unit — endpoint + two middleware swaps land together.

1. **`fix(p6-auth): route SSR session check through useRequestFetch + /api/internal/auth/me`**
   - Adds `server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts`.
   - Rewrites `require-auth.ts` and `require-onboard.ts` SSR branches to use `useRequestFetch()` against the new endpoint.
   - Drops the now-dead `toFetchHeaders` helper from `require-onboard.ts`.

### Pre-push

`bun run validate` locally; only push once green. No `--no-verify`.

### PR

- Don't open a new PR. Push to `fix/p6-auth-ssr-baseurl` so PR #50 picks up the third commit.
- After pushing, `gh pr edit 50` to:
  - Keep the title (`fix(p6-auth): fix SSR session lookup on direct page load / refresh`) — still accurate.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts",
  "content": "// Thin \"who am I\" probe backing the SSR branch of require-auth /\n// require-onboard. Calls the server-side session helper directly so the\n// middleware does not need to roundtrip through the better-auth Vue\n// client (relative URLs blow up under Node fetch) or through better-auth's\n// own /api/auth/get-session handler (cookies don't reliably survive the\n// plain $fetch → Nitro local-dispatch path during SSR).\n//\n// Returns { user: null } for anonymous callers rather than 401, so the\n// middleware can branch on the user shape instead of catching errors.\n\nexport default defineEventHandler(async (event) => {\n  const user = await getSessionUser(event)\n  return { user: user ?? null }\n})\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/middleware/require-auth.ts",
  "content": "// SSR session lookup goes through `useRequestFetch()` against a thin\n// internal endpoint (`/api/internal/auth/me`) that calls\n// `getSessionUser(event)` directly on the server. `useRequestFetch()`\n// returns `event.$fetch`, which is the h3 `fetchWithEvent` wrapper that\n// auto-forwards the original request's headers — including `cookie` —\n// into the local-dispatched call. Plain `$fetch` does not, and routing\n// through better-auth's own `/api/auth/get-session` handler proved\n// unreliable in this stack.\n//\n// Same JSON-over-internal-endpoint pattern as `require-reviewer.ts`.\n\ntype MeResponse = { user: { id: string } | null }\n\nexport default defineNuxtRouteMiddleware(async (to) => {\n  let hasUser = false\n\n  if (import.meta.server) {\n    try {\n      const { user } = await useRequestFetch()<MeResponse>(\n        \"/api/internal/auth/me\",\n      )\n      hasUser = !!user\n    } catch {\n      hasUser = false\n    }\n  } else {\n    const session = useAuth().useSession()\n    hasUser = !!session.value.data?.user\n  }\n\n  if (!hasUser) {\n    const localePath = useLocalePath()\n    return navigateTo({\n      path: localePath(\"/sign-in\"),\n      query: { next: to.fullPath },\n    })\n  }\n})\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/middleware/require-onboard.ts",
  "content": "// SSR session lookup goes through `useRequestFetch()` against the\n// thin `/api/internal/auth/me` endpoint — see require-auth.ts for the\n// rationale.\n\ntype UserShape = { id: string; defaultDeptId?: string | null }\ntype MeResponse = { user: UserShape | null }\n\nexport default defineNuxtRouteMiddleware(async (to) => {\n  let rawUser: UserShape | null = null\n\n  if (import.meta.server) {\n    try {\n      const { user } = await useRequestFetch()<MeResponse>(\n        \"/api/internal/auth/me\",\n      )\n      rawUser = user\n    } catch {\n      rawUser = null\n    }\n  } else {\n    const session = useAuth().useSession()\n    rawUser = (session.value.data?.user as UserShape | undefined) ?? null\n  }\n\n  if (!rawUser) return\n  if (rawUser.defaultDeptId) return\n\n  const localePath = useLocalePath()\n  if (to.path === localePath(\"/onboard\")) return\n  return navigateTo(localePath(\"/onboard\"))\n})\n"
}

In practice it\ndoesn't — hard-refreshing /en/publish (require-auth + require-onboard)\nstill bounces the user back to /sign-in. Either Nitro's local-dispatch\npath doesn't preserve the Cookie header through fetchNodeRequestHandler,\nor better-auth's handler rejects the in-process request for another\nreason. Either way the design — re-routing SSR through better-auth's\nHTTP surface — is wrong.\n\nAdd a thin /api/internal/auth/me endpoint that calls getSessionUser(event)\ndirectly server-side, and have the middlewares hit it via\nuseRequestFetch(). useRequestFetch() returns event.\\$fetch, which is the\nh3 fetchWithEvent wrapper — it merges the original request's headers\n(cookie included; h3's ignored-headers list excludes it) into the\nlocal-dispatched call automatically. {
  "command": "gh pr edit 50 --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nHard-refreshing an authenticated page like `/en/admin/approvals` or `/en/publish` first 500-ed (`Failed to parse URL from /api/auth/get-session`), then — after the first two commits — stopped 500-ing but **bounced the user back to `/sign-in`** even with a valid session cookie. Three coupled commits ship in this PR so the refresh actually works:\n\n1. `38a2115` — `app/composables/useAuth.ts`: give better-auth's Vue client an absolute `baseURL` of `useRequestURL().origin` on the server, so Node's `fetch` stops throwing on `/api/auth/get-session`. Kept as defensive coverage for any future SSR caller that still routes through the better-auth Vue client (today only `UserButton.vue`'s reactive `useSession()`, which doesn't fetch).\n2. `b44997d` — `app/middleware/require-auth.ts` + `app/middleware/require-onboard.ts`: stop using the better-auth Vue client for SSR session reads. *(Superseded by commit 3 — kept for history.)*\n3. `0427ef1` — Add `server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts` that wraps `getSessionUser(event)` from `server/utils/auth.ts`, and have the SSR branch of both middlewares hit it via `useRequestFetch()`. `useRequestFetch()` returns `event.$fetch` (h3's `fetchWithEvent`), which automatically forwards the original request's headers — including `Cookie`, since h3's ignored-headers list excludes it — into the local-dispatched call. The middleware never touches the better-auth Vue client on the server again, and SSR session reads never bounce through better-auth's HTTP handler.\n\n## Why this shape\n\n- **Why a dedicated `/api/internal/auth/me` instead of pointing `useRequestFetch()` at `/api/auth/get-session`.** Routing through better-auth's own HTTP handler from SSR was the original problem; I have no evidence it handles in-process dispatch cleanly, and the failure mode (silent \"not signed in\" on refresh) is exactly what bit the user. Going through our own endpoint that calls `getSessionUser(event)` directly is the proven path — it's exactly what `/api/internal/admin/me` already does for `require-reviewer.ts`.\n- **Why three commits not one.** They're the actual diagnostic arc: kill the 500, kill the client self-call, then route through a known-good helper. Squashing would lose the history of what each layer actually fixed.\n\n## Test plan\n\n- [x] `bun run validate` — lint + typecheck + 367/367 tests green locally.\n- [ ] **Manual: the user's reported scenario.** Sign in (e.g. `amy@agentcenter.dev`), navigate to `/en/publish`, **hard-refresh** — the page should render normally, not 500 and not bounce to `/sign-in`.\n- [ ] Manual: hard-refresh `/en/admin/approvals` signed-in — should render.\n- [ ] Manual: hard-refresh `/en/publish` signed-out — should redirect to `/en/sign-in?next=/en/publish`, no 500.\n- [ ] Manual: sign in as a user with `defaultDeptId = null`, hard-refresh any authenticated page — should redirect to `/en/onboard` via `require-onboard`.\n- [ ] Manual: spot check client-side nav — `UserButton`'s `useSession()` updates after sign-in / sign-out.\n\n## Follow-up (intentional, not in this PR)\n\n`app/middleware/require-reviewer.ts` and `app/middleware/require-super-admin.ts` still use the plain-`$fetch` + manual-cookie-header pattern that commit `b44997d` introduced. They'd silently redirect to `/` rather than `/sign-in` on a hard-refresh, which is why they haven't surfaced as a bug — same fix shape (`useRequestFetch()`) should land in a follow-up PR after this one verifies.\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -3",
  "description": "Update PR #50 description to reflect the three-commit arc"
}

1	// Thin "who am I" probe backing the SSR branch of require-auth /
2	// require-onboard. Calls the server-side session helper directly so the
3	// middleware does not need to roundtrip through the better-auth Vue
4	// client (relative URLs blow up under Node fetch) or through better-auth's
5	// own /api/auth/get-session handler (cookies don't reliably survive the
6	// plain $fetch → Nitro local-dispatch path during SSR).
7	//
8	// Returns { user: null } for anonymous callers rather than 401, so the
9	// middleware can branch on the user shape instead of catching errors.
10	
11	export default defineEventHandler(async (event) => {
12	  const user = await getSessionUser(event)
13	  return { user: user ?? null }
14	})
15	
```

> AGENT

The `publish` and `profile` failures point at one of two things: either Nuxt didn't HMR the new `server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts` server route (new server routes typically need a dev-server restart) or `useRequestFetch()` isn't doing what I think inside middleware. Approval queue + reviewer matrix have a separate cause — they also gate through `require-reviewer`, which I deferred to a follow-up and is still on the old broken pattern.

Let me knock both out: extend the fix to cover all four middlewares so the admin pages work, then ask you to restart dev so it picks up the new endpoint.

1	// Gate the /admin/reviewers page. Requires the caller to hold a
2	// `superAdmin` membership. Authentication is enforced separately via

4	export default defineNuxtRouteMiddleware(async () => {
5	  const localePath = useLocalePath()
6	  try {
7	    const headers = import.meta.server
8	      ? useRequestHeaders(["cookie"])
9	      : {}

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/middleware/require-reviewer.ts",
  "content": "// Gate the /admin/approvals page. Requires the caller to either hold a\n// `superAdmin` membership or be assigned to at least one (tier, subCat)\n// cell in the reviewer matrix. Authentication is enforced separately via\n// `require-auth` — apply this middleware after it.\n//\n// On the server, `useRequestFetch()` returns `event.$fetch`, which auto-\n// forwards the original request's cookies into the local-dispatched call;\n// plain `$fetch` does not, and silently treats the caller as anonymous,\n// which would bounce a real reviewer to `/` on hard-refresh.\n\ntype AdminMe = { isSuperAdmin: boolean; isReviewer: boolean }\n\nexport default defineNuxtRouteMiddleware(async () => {\n  const localePath = useLocalePath()\n  const ssrFetch = import.meta.server ? useRequestFetch() : $fetch\n  try {\n    const me = await ssrFetch<AdminMe>(\"/api/internal/admin/me\")\n    if (!me.isReviewer) {\n      return navigateTo(localePath(\"/\"))\n    }\n  } catch {\n    return navigateTo(localePath(\"/sign-in\"))\n  }\n})\n"
}

**Important for your verification:** the previous commit (`0427ef1`) added a brand-new server route at `server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts`. Nuxt dev's HMR for *new* server routes is unreliable — you most likely need to **kill `bun run dev` and restart it** before testing, otherwise `useRequestFetch()("/api/internal/auth/me")` will 404 and the middleware will silently treat you as anon. > AGENT

Following the diagnose discipline. **Phase 1: feedback loop**, **Phase 3: hypotheses before testing**.

**Ranked hypotheses** (each falsifiable by the probe script below):

1. **H1 — `useRequestFetch()` falls back to plain `globalThis.$fetch`** (cookies not auto-forwarded) because `useRequestEvent()` returns undefined inside a Nuxt 4 route middleware. Predicts: direct curl to `/api/internal/auth/me` returns a user, but a full-page refresh bounces.
2. **H2 — `getSessionUser(event)` doesn't see the cookie inside the SSR-synthesized event** (node-mock-http's `IncomingMessage` headers don't round-trip through h3's `getRequestHeaders`). Predicts: refresh bounces, *and* the in-process middleware-log shows `cookie=undefined`, but a real HTTP curl from outside still works.
3. **H3 — `auth.api.getSession({ headers })` itself doesn't validate when given only headers** (better-auth needs a Request, not a Headers object). Predicts: direct curl to `/api/internal/auth/me` returns `{ user: null }` even with a known-good cookie.
4. **H4 — the new `server/api/internal/auth/me.get.ts` route isn't picked up by HMR** (404), so middleware throws and the catch treats it as anon. Predicts: direct curl to `/api/internal/auth/me` returns 404.
5. **H5 — the dev server is serving stale middleware code** (working tree out of sync, or HMR cache lost the rewrites). Predicts: H4-like symptoms plus `git log` on the user's working tree may not include the recent commits.

Probes will bisect these in one shot. Building the loop now.

1	// Gate the /admin/approvals page. Requires the caller to either hold a
2	// `superAdmin` membership or be assigned to at least one (tier, subCat)
3	// cell in the reviewer matrix. Authentication is enforced separately via
4	// `require-auth` — apply this middleware after it.
5	//
6	// On the server, `useRequestFetch()` returns `event.$fetch`, which auto-
7	// forwards the original request's cookies into the local-dispatched call;
8	// plain `$fetch` does not, and silently treats the caller as anonymous,
9	// which would bounce a real reviewer to `/` on hard-refresh.
10	
11	type AdminMe = { isSuperAdmin: boolean; isReviewer: boolean }
12	
13	export default defineNuxtRouteMiddleware(async () => {
14	  const localePath = useLocalePath()
15	  const ssrFetch = import.meta.server ? useRequestFetch() : $fetch
16	  try {
17	    const me = await ssrFetch<AdminMe>("/api/internal/admin/me")
18	    if (!me.isReviewer) {
19	      return navigateTo(localePath("/"))
20	    }
21	  } catch {
22	    return navigateTo(localePath("/sign-in"))
23	  }
24	})
25

1	// SSR session lookup goes through `useRequestFetch()` against a thin
2	// internal endpoint (`/api/internal/auth/me`) that calls
3	// `getSessionUser(event)` directly on the server. `useRequestFetch()`
4	// returns `event.$fetch`, which is the h3 `fetchWithEvent` wrapper that
5	// auto-forwards the original request's headers — including `cookie` —
6	// into the local-dispatched call. Plain `$fetch` does not, and routing
7	// through better-auth's own `/api/auth/get-session` handler proved
8	// unreliable in this stack.
9	//
10	// Same JSON-over-internal-endpoint pattern as `require-reviewer.ts`.
11	
12	type MeResponse = { user: { id: string } | null }
13	
14	export default defineNuxtRouteMiddleware(async (to) => {
15	  let hasUser = false
16	
17	  if (import.meta.server) {
18	    const ssrFetch = useRequestFetch()
19	    console.log("[DEBUG-a4f2] require-auth ssr branch", {
20	      to: to.fullPath,
21	      requestFetchIsGlobalFallback: ssrFetch === globalThis.$fetch,
22	    })
23	    try {
24	      const res = await ssrFetch<MeResponse>("/api/internal/auth/me")
25	      console.log("[DEBUG-a4f2] require-auth ssr got", {
26	        userId: res?.user?.id ?? null,
27	      })
28	      hasUser = !!res?.user
29	    } catch (err) {
30	      console.log("[DEBUG-a4f2] require-auth ssr threw", {
31	        err: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err),
32	      })
33	      hasUser = false
34	    }
35	  } else {
36	    const session = useAuth().useSession()
37	    hasUser = !!session.value.data?.user
38	  }
39	
40	  if (!hasUser) {
41	    const localePath = useLocalePath()
42	    return navigateTo({

id: toolu_01XaNWkvHBfNSeU9NSv7XdQC
```
1	// Gate the /admin/approvals page. Requires the caller to either hold a
2	// `superAdmin` membership or be assigned to at least one (tier, subCat)
3	// cell in the reviewer matrix. Authentication is enforced separately via
4	// `require-auth` — apply this middleware after it.
5	//
6	// On the server, `useRequestFetch()` returns `event.$fetch`, which auto-
7	// forwards the original request's cookies into the local-dispatched call;
8	// plain `$fetch` does not, and silently treats the caller as anonymous,
9	// which would bounce a real reviewer to `/` on hard-refresh.
10	
11	type AdminMe = { isSuperAdmin: boolean; isReviewer: boolean }
12	
13	export default defineNuxtRouteMiddleware(async () => {
14	  const localePath = useLocalePath()
15	  try {
16	    const me = (await useRequestFetch()(
17	      "/api/internal/admin/me",
18	    )) as AdminMe
19	    if (!me.isReviewer) {
20	      return navigateTo(localePath("/"))
21	    }
22	  } catch {
23	    return navigateTo(localePath("/sign-in"))
24	  }
25	})
26	
```

  "new_string": "  try {\n    // Cast to (url) => Promise<unknown> sidesteps Nuxt's route-discriminated\n    // $fetch type inference, which trips \"excessive stack depth\" when\n    // useRequestFetch()'s return type is a union with the global $fetch.\n    const fetchAny = useRequestFetch() as (url: string) => Promise<unknown>\n    const me = (await fetchAny(\"/api/internal/admin/me\")) as AdminMe\n    if (!me.isReviewer) {"
}

Massive finding from the logs: **SSR session lookup works perfectly.** `userId: 'user-ben'` comes back from the me-handler. So the middleware should NOT be redirecting from SSR. Which means the bounce must be happening **on the client after hydration**, not on the server.

That reframes the entire bug. Let me look at the client branch of `require-auth` and how better-auth's `useSession()` initializes client-side.

import { useAuthQuery } from "./query.mjs";
import { createSessionRefreshManager } from "./session-refresh.mjs";
import { atom, onMount } from "nanostores";
//#region src/client/session-atom.ts
function getSessionAtom($fetch, options) {
	const $signal = atom(false);
	const session = useAuthQuery($signal, "/get-session", $fetch, { method: "GET" });
	let broadcastSessionUpdate = () => {};
	onMount(session, () => {
		const refreshManager = createSessionRefreshManager({
			sessionAtom: session,
			sessionSignal: $signal,
			$fetch,
			options
		});
		refreshManager.init();
		broadcastSessionUpdate = refreshManager.broadcastSessionUpdate;
		return () => {
			refreshManager.cleanup();
		};
	});
	return {
		session,
		$sessionSignal: $signal,
		broadcastSessionUpdate: (trigger) => broadcastSessionUpdate(trigger)
	};
}
//#endregion
export { getSessionAtom };
```

> AGENT

**ROOT CAUSE FOUND.** Look at the log timestamps and the doubled `me handler entered` lines — SSR works perfectly. The user is found server-side. The page renders. Then **Nuxt re-runs the same route middleware on the client during hydration**, the `else` branch runs `useAuth().useSession()`, that's better-auth's reactive atom which **is empty on first mount** (it fetches asynchronously). The middleware sees `data: null` synchronously → `hasUser = false` → redirects to `/sign-in` from the client.

The fix is to use `await auth.getSession()` (the imperative API) on the client branch instead of the reactive atom — `getSession()` actually fetches and resolves before the middleware decides.

Let me apply the fix and keep the logs in place so you can verify.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01G2TJppxvVfHgpMmyWW6BcD
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/app/middleware/require-auth.ts",
  "old_string": "  } else {\n    const session = useAuth().useSession()\n    hasUser = !!session.value.data?.user\n  }",
  "new_string": "  } else {\n    // After SSR rehydration / client-side nav, Nuxt re-runs route\n    // middleware on the client. better-auth's `useSession()` is a\n    // reactive nanostore that fetches *asynchronously on mount*, so on\n    // first read it's still `{ data: null, isPending: true }` and a\n    // synchronous check would falsely conclude \"no user\" and bounce to\n    // sign-in. `getSession()` is the imperative form — it awaits the\n    // actual /api/auth/get-session call and returns the resolved data.\n    const auth = useAuth()\n    try {\n      const result = await auth.getSession()\n      hasUser = !!result?.data?.user\n      console.log(\"[DEBUG-a4f2] require-auth client\", {\n        hasUser,\n        userId: result?.data?.user?.id ?? null,\n      })\n    } catch (err) {\n      console.log(\"[DEBUG-a4f2] require-auth client threw\", {\n        err: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err),\n      })\n      hasUser = false\n    }\n  }"
}

## Verify

In your browser, hard-refresh `/en/publish`. If my theory is right, you should now stay on the page, and your dev console should show **three** `[DEBUG-a4f2]` lines per refresh:

1. `require-auth ssr branch …` (server)
2. `me handler entered …` + `me handler result { userId: 'user-ben' }` (server, in-process call)
3. `require-auth ssr got { userId: 'user-ben' }` (server)
4. **`require-auth client { hasUser: true, userId: 'user-ben' }`** (this is the new one — client hydration branch)

If `require-auth client` shows `hasUser: true` and the page renders, we're fixed. If it shows `hasUser: false` or throws, the imperative `getSession()` call has its own problem and I'll diagnose further.

Try `/en/profile` too. The `admin/approvals` and `admin/reviewers` pages still need a separate fix (their `require-reviewer` / `require-super-admin` calls `useRequestFetch()` server-side which is fine, but the client branch wasn't rewritten yet — say so if those still bounce and I'll mirror the fix there).
